Archived bugs from [port_bugs.md](../port_bugs.md). IDs match the `#` column there (permanent, never reused). Cite as `port_bugs.md #NNN`.

Status meanings: CLOSED = user verified the fix. REFUTED = not a bug / DOS does the same / by design.

**Agents: do not read this file whole.** It is history, not a spec. Look a row
up only when you already have its id: `grep -n '| NNN |' docs/archive/bugs_closed.md`,
then read just those lines. See [README.md](README.md) read discipline.

Full pre-2026-09-05 resolution text: [bugs_resolutions_full_2026-09-05.md](bugs_resolutions_full_2026-09-05.md).

| 24 | CLOSED | ↳ (earlier pass) Indian alarm isn't shown. The tier and colours are DOS's (`FUN_112b_0790`): the DS:0x54f6 grudge word `>> 5` clamped 0..3, forced to 3 once `alarm_by_player` passes 0x4a, coloured 10/11/14/12 by tier. |
| 25 | CLOSED | Missing ability to select which unit is active (blinking) with the mouse. | Fixed. A press-and-release on the same map tile used to only re-centre the view when a unit was already selected, so there was no way to activate a different one. |
| 26 | CLOSED | On colony founding, a large swath of fog is revealed. It looks like Coronado's bonus is applied even without him. | Fixed — it *was* Coronado's bonus. DOS `FUN_364b_1dd6` runs `for n in 0..3: if has_ff(n, 6) then FUN_13f1_00a6(colony, n)`; the port called the ±5 reveal unconditionally. |
| 27 | CLOSED | Units loaded onto ships are shown in the transport view in colonies. They should not be; no non-transports should be in that view. Two more problems: 1. On bringing a loaded unit into a colony, they are automatically disembarked and have no orders (and can move if they had any moves left to begin with). | Fixed. The colony hold strip no longer draws passenger sprites — goods only. Docking now puts everyone ashore on *every* arrival path, not just an explicit player move: `units_try_move` disembarks a transport's passengers whenever it enters a friendly colony, with no orders and their remaining moves intact. |

| 33 | CLOSED | "Iroquis accept conversion at Jamestown" is a bizarre popup I've never seen. Conversion is done by establishing a mission in a village, and then sometimes indians come to a colony and spawn a convert in the colony. Also, a village with a mission that is attacked can spawn converts. Check DOS for details. | Fixed. That popup came from an invented per-turn pulse where a human's Missionary standing next to a village silently established a mission and announced it; first contact also handed out a free mission. |
| 34 | CLOSED | When SoL membership reaches 50% there's a persistent popup with a choice to rebel or not. I've not seen this in DOS, except maybe at 100%. That popup is normally only shown when the player selects game->Declare Independence. It should not be shown unless you can show that DOS spawns it spontaneously at some threshold of SoL. | Fixed — could not show it, because DOS does not. `FUN_43f7_2564` has no call site anywhere in the decompile; it is reached only from the MENU.TXT @GAME "DECLARE INDEPENDENCE" command. |
| 35 | CLOSED | Clicking anywhere while in a multiple-choices popup selects the current one. It should do nothing instead. Only clicking one of the two options should be considered a choice. | Fixed. On a CHOICE dialog only a click on an option row decides anything; clicks on the body, the chrome, or outside the dialog are swallowed. ESC and right-click still cancel. |
| 36 | CLOSED | "Tea Party" never actually happens the "Tea" part is always substituted by some actual cargo Colony models. | Fixed. GAME.TXT's `{%STRING3 Party}` is built by `FUN_38fd_3dc8` as colony name + " " + cargo name, so it reads e.g. "Jamestown Tobacco Party!"; the port hardcoded `%STRING3` to "Tea". Now built the same way and used for @TAXOPTIONS' "Hold '… Party.'" row, the @TEAPARTY body, and the popup title. |
| 37 | CLOSED | Clicking the map, even a colony, ends the turn if the End of Turn marker in the sidebar is active. Only clicking the sidebar should then be considered an end of turn press. Clicking a colony should almost always be considered as an instruction to open up the colony UI. | Fixed. The map-viewport End-of-Turn confirm is gone; a click there keeps its normal meaning (open a colony, activate a unit, start a go-to). Only the right-hand sidebar click confirms the turn. |
| 38 | REFUTED | "Throwing away excess cargo" should never happen just from filling up from production. If a colony produces more goods than its stores allow, that's normally ignored. Every 100 units of a cargo is reason for a "colony has a new load of <cargo> available" popup instead (gated behind the appropriate notification setting). | Fixed, and it matches DOS exactly. `FUN_364b_0688` reports a loss only for the part of the overflow that predates this turn's production (`if (production < overflow)`); either branch ends clamped at capacity, so production overflow is a silent clamp with no @SPOIL. |
| 39 | CLOSED | Indians begging for food ask for 0 food. They never do than in DOS. | Fixed. GAME.TXT's accept row is `"We offer you {%NUMBER0} of our {%NUMBER1 food} as a sign of friendship."` and the port set neither number, so both rendered 0 — and the choice rows were handed to the popup straight from the catalog without any token substitution at all. |
| 40 | CLOSED | Converts are shown with their proper graphic in colony UI, but as free colonists on the overland map. | Fixed. DOS has one icon rule for both views: `FUN_112b_0060` routes *every* @UNIT type 0 (Colonists) unit through `FUN_112b_0002(profession)`, whose jump table (CS:0x14, case 8) sends profession 0x1b to icon 0x43 — sprite 66 here, exactly the one `units_job_icon_sprite` already returned for the colony screen. |
| 41 | CLOSED | Cargo Party ie. boycotting a good should always pick a cargo the selected colony has. You can't dump 0 tons of something in protest. | Fixed, and DOS agrees. `FUN_38fd_3dc8` fills `aiStack_cc[c]` with the largest stock of cargo c across the human's colonies and skips every cargo whose entry stayed 0, both when summing the roulette weights and when walking them. |
| 42 | CLOSED | Mission icon on Indian villages should be a cross, of a color dependent on nation and missionary skill. Check DOS. It's not an exclamation mark. | Fixed — it is a cross, and the colour rule is nation plus Jesuit. Ghidra drops the register arguments to `FUN_1b9e_000a` (the rect fill: colour and height on the stack, x in AX, y in DX, width in BX), so the geometry came out of the raw instruction stream at CODE_5:112b:0afd..0b8c — a 5x6 black pad, a 1x4 vertical bar at pad+2 and a 3x1 horizontal bar at pad+1 one row down. |
| 43 | CLOSED | Indian alarm icon, the exclamation mark, should be centered, and I'm pretty sure the colors are a bit wrong vs DOS. | Fixed; the whole thing now comes off the disassembly rather than a reading of the decompile. Position is DOS's fixed anchor — first mark at tile_x+6, baseline tile_y+4, each mark stepping 2px (they overlap, being 3px wide) — instead of the previous centred row whose width drifted with the count. |
| 44 | CLOSED | Name new world popup fails to stop execution until it is resolved, like other popups. | Fixed together with the turn-processing row below. DOS runs every popup as a blocking call, so the code behind one simply does not run until it is answered; here dialogs are asynchronous, so `game_after_unit_action` kept going after `game_try_prompt_landho` opened the @LANDHO name entry — handing control to the next unit (and, with the End of Turn option off, ending the turn) from behind the prompt. |
| 45 | CLOSED | Returning a ship to Europe immediately opens the European Status. It should not. Ships ARRIVING in Europe should open the status screen. Check DOS for details. | Fixed. ORDERS → Return to Europe had its own copy of the sail-away code ending in `game_try_enter_europe`; the H command and the Go-To-onto-a-lane path (`game_ship_sail_to_europe`) never did that. |
| 46 | CLOSED | Founding Father pedia pages are missing background (should be wood), and the pallette is messed up too. | Fixed, and the two halves had different causes. **Background:** the Colonizopedia *article* view cleared to black — only the category list blitted `WOODPANL.PIK` — so every article, not just a Father's, drew on bare black; the panel is now blitted for articles too. |
| 47 | CLOSED | The only place that time/turn processing is allowed to proceed is the overland map, and only if there ISN'T an active popup. Yet another example of this bug is turns apparently being able to process while in European Status. Things are allowed to happen in places other than the overland map, but then they are always in response to the player doing something. The game should pretty much never proceed with time/turn processing while player is just viewing another screen or a popup. | Fixed structurally rather than case by case. New `game_turn_flow_allowed`: the unit cycle and the end-of-turn processor may only run with the overland map on screen (not menu / Europe / colony / report / pedia / atlas / Hall of Fame / exploits / new-game wizard) and nothing over it — `game_modal_… |
| 48 | CLOSED | Colonists embarked on ships arriving from or traveling to the new world on the European Status need to be visible alongside ships. | 49 | CLOSED | Colony population numbers missing from colony icons on overland map. Check DOS for where, what's counted and what color. | Fixed from DOS `FUN_112b_0c64` (Ghidra drops that function's register arguments, so the geometry was read off `CODE_5:112b:0ddb..0e79`). |
| 50 | CLOSED | If you have a ship with a loaded unit, and you, via the popup menu cancel the orders of that loaded unit, and the unit has remaining moves, that unit should be available to move. This is particularly important in coastal tiles, since this allows disembarking units on board a ship that's out of moves (and hence incapable of triggering the landfall popup). | Fixed. Boarding parks a passenger at `moves_left = 0` as a "don't offer this one" flag while DOS's own spent byte is still zero (a full allotment) — `units_unload_passenger` already knew that and restored the allotment for its move charge, but the tile-stack picker woke a passenger with a bare `orders = 0`, leaving it awake with no MP so every move gate (`game_try_unit_move`, the arrow-key handler) refused it. |
| 51 | CLOSED | Colonists working buildings in colony UI stand too far apart. If the building is wide enough, have them stand centered horizontally on the building, right next to each other as per their sprite widths. If the building is too narrow for that, squeeze them as needed. | Fixed. `colony_screen_icon_strip_layout` spread the icons across the host rect's whole width (first at the left edge, last at the right). |
| 52 | CLOSED | Indians begging for food should ask for half of the colony stores. The popup seems to read the amount of food at the colony wrong too. | FIXED, and the two branches of the choice were also the wrong way round. Re-read of DOS `FUN_5bfb_022e`: the choice returns `local_c`, and it is `local_c == 2` — GAME.TXT's second row, "We offer you {%NUMBER0} of our {%NUMBER1 food}" — that subtracts `colony->food >> 1`. |
| 53 | CLOSED | Foreign Affairs report seems to think that the player nation was withdrawn from the new world. | FIXED. The report is right — DOS prints @MISC 190 "(Withdrawn from New World)" for whichever block matches `head.crown_nation_id` (DS:0x53d2) — but new games were writing that field as 0, i.e. |
| 54 | CLOSED | I think the sound when you assign someone to work a town hall is meant to be when you assign them to a church/cathedral. | FIXED, and it explains the silver-mine row below too. DOS `FUN_2f2b_2f3e` — the whole assign-colonist path — plays exactly one sound, the `0x8024` chord, and it is gated (2f2b:33dd..33fc): the occupation being handed out must be @JOB 0x10 Preacher or @JOB 0x18 Missionary. |
| 55 | CLOSED | Why can't I fortify a ship in coastals waters? Seems to happen only sometimes. Investigate; I might be wrong. | FIXED, and you were right. DOS's Fortify command is `FUN_2b5a_1112`: its only type test is `type < 0x0d || type > 0x12` — the six ship types — and that gate guards the *adjacent-foreign-power war prompt* the command runs first, not the order. |
| 56 | CLOSED | Paul Revere is absent from Continental Congress second page even when he's supposed to be present. Francis Drake, too. DOS `FUN_4345_01a6` (4345:01b5..0246) is a plain 25-iteration loop: take the next father id from the draw-order table at DS:0x123a, skip it unless this nation owns it, build "CC-" + a leading "0" below 10 + the id (DS strings 0x1234 / 0x1238), load that sh… |
| 57 | CLOSED | Clicking a lone fortified (or sentried) unit on the map (not on a colony tile) should cancel the order. Verify against DOS when clicking on units cancels orders. There's doubless a bunch of gates. | FIXED. DOS's click-on-tile handler (2b5a:1b9b..1dda) ends every path that makes a unit active with `unit[active].orders = 0`, and its single-unit branch clears the order before that as well — clicking is how you take a unit off its post. |
| 58 | CLOSED | Units on European docks should be printed 2px higher. Also, they should never have the "multiple units on stack" allegiance-orders presentation. Always singular. | FIXED. `EUROPE_DOCK_Y` 140 -> 138 (the hit-test in `europe_dock_menu_hit` shares the constant, so clicks moved with the art), and the dock row no longer passes `show_stack`. |
| 59 | CLOSED | Orders-allegiance chrome, when it uses the "multiple units here" presentation is incorrect for common units, like colonists and soldiers. The background rectangle indicating more units should be upper-left not bottom-right. | FIXED, and you had the direction exactly right. DOS `FUN_112b_01ba`'s corner switch is on the raw instruction stream (Ghidra drops the rect call's register args): the three named corners all place the stack rect with a -2/+2-family offset, and the fall-through corner — the one every plain Colonis… |
| 60 | CLOSED | Units loaded onto transports in European Status view should be listed as having Sentry orders on their chrome, not "no orders" like now. | FIXED. `europe_render_transit_box` passed `UNITS_ORDER_NONE` for passengers on the reasoning that cargo has no orders. It does: DOS `FUN_38fd_0718` stamps `+0x314c = 1` (Sentry) on every unit it puts on the dock, and boarding preserves it. |
| 61 | CLOSED | Ships arrive in Europe from the New World way too fast compared to DOS. Same for reaching the New World from Europe. | FIXED against your 2-turn observation (order / End Turn: nothing / End Turn: arrives). The counter itself was already right: `FUN_48d3_0002` returns 1 for an ordinary crossing (2 on a ~10% roll that needs >2 ships and no Magellan), both set-sail paths write it to the unit's `turns_worked`, and `FUN_48d3_03d0` decrements once per tick and advances the lane at 0. |
| 62 | CLOSED | Something is very wrong with the Foreign Affairs report regarding Rebels vs Tories numbers. Despite having a colony with 2 pops and 100% SoL, I had 0 rebels in Foreign Affairs. Foreign affairs seems not to count units loaded onto ships as actual colonists for Rebels vs Tories either, but does appear to count those on European docks. | FIXED, two separate causes, both confirmed against DOS. **0 Rebels:** the report's formula was already right — DOS `3f41:2902` reads `[nation*0x13c + 0x8821]` (that is `nation + 0x19`, `rebel_sentiment`), IMULs it by `census_pop_proxy`, IDIVs by 100, and Tories are the remainder — but nothing in the port ever *wrote* that byte, so it sat at 0 for the whole campaign and everyone was a Tory at any SoL. |
| 63 | CLOSED | Ships docking into colonies should auto-disembark their loaded units. This does not always happen when you sail a loaded ship into a colony. I don't know why it sometimes happens but other times does not. | Partly fixed; the "sometimes" half is not reproduced. Two real defects found and fixed. (a) The auto-disembark shared a condition with the wagon/ship MP-forfeit rule: it was gated on `units_is_transport`, which for a ship answers "does this hull have goods holds" — a different question from "is anyone riding in it". |
| 64 | CLOSED | Hardy Pioneers who appear on docks should always have 100 tools on them. Now they appear without tools. | FIXED, and the port was dropping more than the tools. | 65 | CLOSED | Putting a colonist to mine silver produces the church sound effect. WTF. | FIXED with the town-hall row above — same root cause. The `0x8024` chord is DOS's *church* chord, gated in `FUN_2f2b_2f3e` on the new occupation being Preacher (@JOB 0x10) or Missionary (@JOB 0x18); the port played it on every assignment. |
| 66 | CLOSED | Incomplete cargo stacks in ships in European Status view suggest they have a full stack. Ie. they are colored, but should be grey. | FIXED. ICONS.SS carries both sets — coloured at 22, grey at 38 — and the map sidebar and the colony transport pane both already switch on a full 100. The European Status hold strip was the one place that always blitted the coloured icon regardless of amount. |
| 67 | CLOSED | European Status is missing "equip with tools"/"horses"/"arm with muskets" options when clicking a colonist on the docks. Check DOS. | FIXED — the whole @ARMOPTIONS menu is in. The port's four invented rows (None / Don't board / Board next / Move to front) are replaced by GAME.TXT's twelve, titled from `@EUROPEARM`, transcribed from DOS `FUN_38fd_37xx`: the price/quantity setup at 38fd:3745..3830 (Muskets @CARGO 0x0f at 50, Tool… |
| 68 | CLOSED | Recruit popup on European Status can't be operated by mouse, only keyboard. Neither can Purchase and Train popups. Fix that. | FIXED. The Europe input path returned early for every event while a menu was open, so only the key handlers below it ever ran. |
| 69 | CLOSED | Train Popup on European Status view should list the professions in the same order as DOS. | FIXED. `FUN_38fd_41ce` collects every @JOB with a positive hire cost in job order — which is what this port was displaying — and then hands the cost array and the parallel job-id array to `FUN_291f_0ed0` -> `FUN_1cf8_000a` before drawing a single row. |
| 70 | CLOSED | The three European popups use the wrong fonts. Check what DOS uses. | FIXED — FONTINTR, not FONTSMAL. All four of those menus go through DOS's one generic list dialog (`FUN_291f_0182` -> `FUN_6f74_32a4`), which takes its font from the far pointer at DS:0x1f9e/0x1fa0. |
| 71 | CLOSED | Orders-allegiance chrome uses incorrect color for the orders symbol. It should be normally black, at least AFAIK. It is a dark orange kind of color instead. | NOT CHANGED — the port already does what DOS does, and I could measure it rather than argue it. DOS `FUN_112b_01ba` (112b:06e6..0708) sets the letter ink: orders 1 (Sentry) or 6 (Fortified) take the nation's @COUNTRY colour **minus 8** for a European (`local_2a < 4`) or a flat 8 for a native; every other order takes 0, black. |

The DOS force row goes through the shared icon-row drawer — the 3f41 page builder brackets exactly that line with `DS:0x70 = 1` (the FUN_1097_0174 numbers flag, same engine as the colony panes) — which packs icons at their natural width and shrinks the step only when the run would overflow its cell. |
| 118 | CLOSED | Fog edges still black. | FIXED. | 134 | CLOSED | Starting gold on lower difficulties is zero. | 179 | CLOSED | Popups must block ALL processing until answered — there is not a single non-blocking popup in the whole game. | 180 | CLOSED | REF landing is too early. They currently land the same turn as the declaration of independence is signed. They should wait one more turn before doing so. | FIXED. Unit-tested: declare turn lands nothing, next turn lands. |
| 181 | CLOSED | REF landing doesn't update the map properly. See REF_bugs.SAV. | FIXED (REF_bugs.SAV — the whole wave had piled onto one occupied tile). The landing now prefers EMPTY adjacent tiles: if any candidate tile is free, only free tiles are used and nothing is seized; the DOS seize-what-stands-there landing remains only for the blockade case where every adjacent tile… |
| 183 | CLOSED | REF still uses French appearance. | FIXED for real this time. The earlier white override patched unit_chrome_nation_color, but the MAIN MAP renders through the palette-adapting wrapper, which reads the nation RGB table directly and never consulted it — so the map stayed French blue. |
| 184 | CLOSED | Foreign intervention in the war of independence does not appear to be implemented at all yet. | FIXED — it was implemented but dead-gated. The bell-pool spend that triggers foreign intervention (and follow-up waves) returns 0 while DS:0x5382's REF-present bit is set, and the port set that bit at the DECLARATION and never cleared it — so the trigger could not fire for the entire war. |
| 185 | CLOSED | REF now correctly white, but still listed as "French". REF uses incorrect unit presentations; REF is composed of Regulars and Cavalry, not Soldiers and Dragoons. | 186 | CLOSED | REF still lands on my units despite having a perfectly good tile to land on, upon which they previously landed forces. | FIXED — the previous fix's "empty" test rejected tiles held by the CROWN'S OWN earlier landings, so a second wave saw its beachhead as occupied and fell through to seizing your units. |
| 187 | CLOSED | The "activate multiple units popup" needs to cancel orders of every unit clicked, on the first click, not the second. As in, you click the stack on the map, popup shows up. | 188 | CLOSED | Fortified units (ie. fortified in a previous turn, not this one) should have all their movement points unspent, and can move on order cancellation and activation. | FIXED per your clarification — the order byte itself is the rule. | 189 | CLOSED | Player Continental Cavalry and Continental Army should use its own graphics, and be distinguishable from veterans/unskilled soldiers. | FIXED. | 190 | CLOSED | Choice of defender seems off. | 193 | CLOSED | Something seems wrong with artillery damage. On being damaged (ie. losing a fight) artillery becomes Damaged Artillery, a weaker unit. I'm not seeing any. | FIXED. | 194 | CLOSED | Dragoons and other mounted units (scouts, mounted braves, mounted warriors, cavalry, continental cavalry) have one attack per turn, no matter how many moves they have remaining. | 195 | CLOSED | First click on a stack should not cancel any orders, it should only open up the stack popup, which shows units there. | 196 | CLOSED | Despite movement having been allegedly implemented visually, I don't see it. | 197 | CLOSED | Attack "bumps" are too fast. Slow them down 3x. (Or 6x if fast move slides are not on.) | FIXED. Per-frame delay 30ms->90ms with Fast Piece Slide on (3x), 45ms->270ms with it off (6x); still 3 frames. |

| 204 | CLOSED | Attacking ships get a +50% attack bonus, just like attacking land units. | FIXED. combat_naval_engage now applies the same ×3/2 attack factor the land formula has always had (atk_strength += atk_strength>>1). Note: this makes equal-strength duels tiebreak to the attacker. |

The move-slide overlay (and the combat lunge) blitted the unit chrome with a NULL palette, falling back to the raw ICONS.SS-native fill index — which the in-game palette repurposes to magenta/pink for the Dutch slot (the exact hazard unit_chrome's own comment documents). |
| 270 | CLOSED | Colony name counter not reset after reload. | FIXED. The per-nation COLONY.TXT name cursor lived only in the session colony pool — the save/load bridge deliberately preserved it across a load, so reloading an older save kept handing out names from wherever the session had wandered. |
| 271 | CLOSED | Fort Orange starves to death despite having enough food production. See: fort_orange_starves.SAV | FIXED (save is campaign2/port_orange_starves.SAV). DOS's starve trigger is DS:0x8e5a = max(0, consumption − stock-at-start − production) — the colony must actually have gone negative this turn. |
| 272 | CLOSED | Merchantman went to Europe to pick up a petty criminal, but vanished en-route back. Any idea what happened to it? See saves: missing_merchantman.SAV, merchantman_sails_back.SAV | FIXED (two defects; the second, found via merchantman_sails_back.SAV, is the real cause for a non-English player). **(a)** The arriving ship spawned via units_spawn_ship_with_cargo, which defaults nation_id to 0 — the arrival never stamped the owner. |
| 273 | CLOSED | Cargo is misaligned with cargo holds in European Status view. We already fixed a similar problem for colony UI. Check that and DOS. | FIXED. Same 9×12 interiors / 12px pitch as colony (EUROPE.PIK, first box at 148,165). Cargo centred in the interior; cover #122 at X−1. |
| 274 | CLOSED | Dragging cargo onto the cargo hold area should load it onto the selected vessel. It sometimes works, sometimes doesn't. Probably some drop-area misalignment issue. | FIXED. Hit-test was a 12×14 box at 146,163 that missed the painted interiors and skipped the 3px gaps. Full 6-slot strip is now a drop target (including gaps); market→hold also auto-selects the first harbor ship if none is selected. |
| 275 | REFUTED | Expert Farmers appear to produce only 1 extra food over non-specialists. Verify with DOS. Pedia is inconsistent on the subject. | VERIFIED, not a bug. Pedia `@JOB0` says experts grow 2 more than other farmers; EXE `FUN_15eb_18ec` gives non-experts the extra farmer `+1` (`local_14==0` at ~11950) and skips that whole crop-improvement block for experts, so expert vs other farmer is **+1**. |
| 276 | CLOSED | In the all_boycotted.SAV all goods appear to be boycotted. Not sure how that happened. Cargoes can only be boycotted one by one and it's very early in the game. Invented Euro-war embargo ORed all 16 `@CARGO` bits on first `declare_war` (Dutch save 1516 / tax 0 / `boycott_bitmap=0xFFFF`, Spain at war). DOS boycotts are king tea-party only. Declare no longer sets the bits; load maps `0xFFFF`→0; peace still lifts leftovers on poisoned saves. |
| 277 | CLOSED | Nations should get their appropriate default new world names. I just got suggested "New England" playing as Dutch. | FIXED. `europe_load` always baked `@COLONYNAME[0]` (New England); load then `europe_reset_campaign()` (nation 0) and LANDHO preferred that stale `colony_region`. LANDHO now seeds `@COLONYNAME[human]`; load/new-game call `europe_set_nation` (Dutch → New Netherlands / Amsterdam / Netherlands). |
| 278 | CLOSED | On loading fog_and_popup.SAV into DOS, no fog is cleared, even though it should be at least around owned units. Also, a popup fires immediately about price changes, even though I haven't taken an end of turn yet. | FIXED (one root cause, two symptoms; the save predates yesterday's human_player stamp fix — it carries head.human_player=0 while its seen bits and control are Dutch, nation 3). |
| 279 | CLOSED | Changing a colonist's loadout (horses, tools, muskets, blessing, etc) should exhaust their moves. It presently does not. | FIXED. game_colony_apply_outside_role only zeroed moves when muskets/horses were taken; now ANY kit change (tools taken or laid down included) sets moves_left=0 — picking the same kit ("No changes") still costs nothing. The colony eject path already zeroed moves for every role (blessing included). |
| 280 | CLOSED | Does DOS double-notify you when cutting down a forest? I got a popup about lumber being added due to chopping a forest, and then another one that deforestation happened. | FIXED — DOS never double-notifies, and in fact never shows @DEFOREST at all: the tag string is absent from VICEROY.EXE (CLEARCUT/DEPLETION/FOODLOW are all present), so that GAME.TXT section is dead data. |
| 281 | CLOSED | I'm pretty sure diplomatic contact with the other Europeans should only occur if their land units or colonies get adjacent to yours. FUN_5bfb_3180 (the adjacent-unit encounter resolver) dispatches FUN_5bfb_153e on any adjacent foreign-nation unit with no land/sea gate, and the first-contact greeting picker explicitly selects @HELLOAHOY when the encountering unit is a ship (vs @HELLOFIRST on land) — a ship-borne first contact has its own dedicated DOS dialog text. |
| 282 | CLOSED | Recruit popup text in European Status appears bolded. That's incorrect. | FIXED. The Europe menu popups (recruit/train/purchase title prose, rows, and cost/footer text) drew with the solid-shade font path, which paints every AA shade as full ink — reads as bold next to every other dialog. |
| 283 | CLOSED | "Sioux march into Amsterdam" is not a thing. Indians do not conquer, they raid, steal stuff, destroy buildings, damage ships, kill colonists, etc. In this instance, an undefended colony was attacked and completely destroyed, which is also incorrect. At worst, Indians kill one colonist at a time. Check DOS! | FIXED, DOS-verified. Decomp (viceroy_unpacked_2.c ~91576, the land-combat colony arm): an Indian winner kills ONE colonist while population > 1; only when the LAST colonist falls is the colony BURNED TO THE GROUND (@INDIANBURNCOLONY "massacre... |
| 284 | CLOSED | Something still wrong with fog in saves. I loaded a game into DOS, and the first turn it was OK. Then one turn later, I got the fog state from the English, but I'm Dutch. | FIXED for real this time — still_foggy.SAV pinned it down (the earlier control-table heal was a different, secondary hazard; this save's control/human_player/nation_turn were all correct). |
| 285 | CLOSED | Clicking an already selected nation and difficulty in the new game setup should confirm selection just the same as Enter does. | FIXED. Nation/difficulty screens: a click on the already-selected entry now activates like Enter; a click elsewhere still only moves the selection (list dialogs — America/map pick — already confirmed on click). |
| 286 | CLOSED | Indian land should be indicated by a totem pole sprite in the colony minimap, and Indians will complain if you try to work those tiles. When you have Peter Minuit, all of those go away. Check how DOS does all of that. | PORTED from FUN_15eb_26e4 (the DOS colony-screen 5x5 Indian-land table) + the tiles[] writer's complaint tail (~overlays.c 8789). |
| 287 | CLOSED | Clicking on main map sidebar minimap while a unit is active should pan just like panning by clicking on the main map itself does. Same idea: user may want to pan to a different location to give a go-to order far away. | FIXED. The minimap click did pan, but the per-frame "active unit must stay on screen" recentre snapped the view straight back — a manual pan (map or minimap) while a unit is active now suspends that unit's auto-recentre until its next action / the next unit hand-off (view_pan_hold_unit). |
| 288 | CLOSED | Food stores depleted should only fire if the food stores are exactly 0. Not just because they decreased. | FIXED. The @FOOD1/@FOOD2 "stores depleted" popup now additionally requires stock exactly 0 (still only while actively losing food); a nonzero-but-shrinking store falls through to the @FOODLOW "stores low" warning instead. |
| 289 | CLOSED | Trying to go-to into an Indian settlement fails, even though it's a valid order. Unit should try to go there and once adjacent, the next move should open up the appropriate "entering indian village" popup, according to what unit that is. | FIXED. units_set_goto already accepted a village destination, but the goto pacing halted the unit adjacent every frame WITHOUT clearing the order (and for attack-capable units would have auto-attacked the dwelling). |
| 290 | CLOSED | Identured servants still offend indians as if they were criminals, when they come to the village to live among the natives. Fix that. | Direct live-among flow verified correct (profession 0x19 reaches the @LEARNSTAY arm like DOS a618; regression test added — servant gets the stay/learn CHOICE, never @LEARNCRIMINAL/@LEARNMASTER). |
| 291 | CLOSED | Indian alarm rises incredibly fast compared to DOS. In DOS I'd have decades before any problems with just one colony of 1 colonist. | FIXED. The port had three fandom-invented per-turn drips layered on top of the DOS mechanism: unit encroachment (+2 alarm+friction per military unit within 2 tiles of a village, every turn), colony encroachment (same +2 per colony within 2 tiles, every turn), and a relation-tick friction +1/turn once alarm hit 40. |
| 292 | CLOSED | Food production formulas look incorrectly ported: port claims expert farmers give +1 extra food, but saves (farming/case1, case2) show +2 over unskilled. New Amsterdam: town square 4 food, criminal 3, expert 6 (case 1); on different tiles farmer 5, criminal 2 (case 2). | FIXED — three real defects found via these saves, though the expert flat bonus itself was already right. The apparent +3/+2 deltas come from rivers: in both cases the expert stands on a river tile and the criminal doesn't (case1 expert: Broadleaf + MAJOR river; case2 expert: Rain forest + minor river). |
| 293 | CLOSED | Added case3 saves (three turns): Fort Orange expert farmer on forest+Game makes 8 food, on a bare hill 4 food. | FIXED — this pair unlocked the whole DOS improvement stack. Port computed 7 and 4-only-by-luck; the missing +1 is FUN_15eb_18ec's UNCONDITIONAL farmer +1 (asm 1c32-1c40: bare job==0 test — applies to experts too, no plow gate). |
| 294 | CLOSED | Fort Nassau is case 4. Mixed forest gives 5 food (base 3 + 2 expert; beavers/furs resource does not grant bonuses, only game does). Mountains yield 0 food, regardless of expertise. | FIXED (one real defect; the Mixed value already matched). Mixed+Beaver expert = 5 was exact under the improvement-stack port (decomposes as base 2 + expert 2 + farmer 1 — same total; Beaver pairs only with Fur Trapper in the 17fa table, so no farmer bonus, as you observed). |
| 295 | CLOSED | Walking a treasure train into a coastal colony should spawn the offer (if any, there are gates) for crown galleon transport immediately. It did not fire for me immediately. IIRC it was the next turn. That's not correct. | FIXED. DOS fires the offer from inside the move handler FUN_465b_0000 (Treasure type 0xa, human-controlled nation, destination colony record +0x1c & 0x40 coastal bit) and calls FUN_5fef_1908 immediately; the port only ran the offer sweep at end of turn. |
| 296 | CLOSED | King's offer to transport treasure popup has messed up text. The options are appended in the text, in addition to being options. | FIXED, shared root cause with the European diplomacy duplication below. The port matched choice rows against a hand-maintained keyword list that never matched the quoted rows of @KINGGALLEON2/3. |
| 297 | CLOSED | High seas/sea lane tiles should be traversable. Currently only the outer edges can be entered by ships. DOS allows sea movement through all of them, without the need to return to Europe. | FIXED. The port turned DOS reason 5 into a hard deny (Need sail order for high seas) for any eastward in-lane step. The DOS UI handler FUN_4720_049e case 4 (asm 0x3FEA6) instead asks @SAILHOME: Yes sails for Europe, No commits the step anyway; under the War of Independence it shows @EUROPENOTLEAVE and still commits. |
| 298 | CLOSED | Is the game loss condition on failing to have colonies by some year wired? | ANSWERED (DOS has it) and FIXED (port did not). DOS FUN_3844_0442 Section B: year >= 1600 (0x640), peacetime, human colonies == 0 gives dialog string 0xf09 = @LOSENOCOLONIES, then LAB_3844_04ec runs the Hall of Fame and clears 0x53c2. |
| 299 | CLOSED | Right-clicking a tile in colony UI should bring up the terrain type pedia entry. Exiting from that entry should go back to colony UI. | FIXED. Right-click on any colony area-view tile (including the centre) now opens the terrain pedia article and returns to the colony screen on exit. |
| 300 | CLOSED | Create trade route (sea route) should include the European port. Edit trade route does already. | FIXED. game_trade_open_dest_picker added the Europe row (and the coastal filter) only for the non-wizard path, so wizard destination 2 never saw it even though the sea/land choice is already made by that stage. Stage-4 wizard now uses trade_create_sea for both. File: game_loop.c. |
| 301 | CLOSED | A ship trying to go to Europe via trade route just goes to the sea lane tile, then returns. Never visits Europe. | FIXED. On reaching the lane tile, game_trade_route_retarget serviced the Europe stop in place (selling and buying while floating on the lane) and aimed at the next stop; the auto-sail check required orders == GOTO, which a TRADE_ROUTE ship never has. |
| 302 | CLOSED | Shift + drag cargo should trigger a popup where you write in how much cargo you want to transfer. Should work on both colony and Europe status, and in and out of transports. | FIXED. Shift+drag now opens the DOS @HOWMUCH1/2/4/5 amount dialog in all four flows: colony warehouse to ship (load), ship hold to warehouse (unload, via new partial-unload colonies_transfer_from_unit_amount, with HOWMUCH_KIND_UNLOAD now honoring the amount), Europe market to ship (buy), and Europe hold to market (sell). |
| 303 | CLOSED | Go-to order to have a colonist walk off a ship onto coast doesn't work. It should. | FIXED. units_set_goto and the goto pacer both required units_is_on_map(), which is false for a unit aboard a ship, so the order was refused outright (Cannot go there). |
| 304 | CLOSED | Totem pole icons indicating tribal land are somehow off from DOS. Perhaps they need 2px shadows like many other icons? | FIXED. The colony-screen totem was hand-drawn rectangles left over from a note that the DOS totem art had not been located. |
| 305 | REFUTED | Indian land should only by in a 1-tile radius around Indian settlements AFAIK. | NOT A BUG. DOS's radius is the tribe tech tier, not a flat 1: FUN_15dc_006a reads nation*0x4e+0x5ad8 and returns 1/1/2/3, and all three call sites test dist <= radius (viceroy_unpacked.c:12850, viceroy_unpacked_2.c:75564 and :75663, the latter being the @INDIANLAND found/clear gate). |
| 306 | CLOSED | Price of Coats changed in response to me selling Sugar. Does that ever happen in DOS? Ie. unrelated price changes? | 307 | REFUTED | Does the port properly change SoL membership and percentages in response to colony population changes? | ANSWERED, matches DOS, no change needed. Membership counts recompute live from current population (sol_count = (pop*sol+50)/100, colony_screen.c:3122, the DOS FUN_15eb_0274 display rule), and percentages track population through the EOT accumulator colony_prod_tick_rebel_accumulators (divide by 6… |
| 308 | CLOSED | Multipurpose-production view does not show lumber shortage when colony isn't producing any lumber, but has some in stock. It should. | FIXED. A carpenter demanding more lumber than the colony produces this tick now shows a red lumber-shortage number even when stock feeds him. |
| 309 | CLOSED | Multipurpose-production view does not show lumber AND hammer shortage when colony isn't producing any lumber and doesn't have any in stock. It should. | FIXED by the same change. With no lumber production and no stock, the pane now shows both the red lumber shortage (capacity minus produced) and a red hammer shortage (capacity minus hammers on the hammers row, which now appears even at 0 hammers banked). |
| 310 | CLOSED | Tax hike popup does not have the animated king flair it should have. Check DOS how that works. | FIXED. The King flair machinery (KING2.SS, 3.9 s hold then 164 ms per frame) and ai_king.c's ai_popup_set_last_portrait(ctx->ai_popups, 8, 0) already existed, but ai_popup_set_last_portrait bailed on tribe > 7 and silently dropped the King (8). |
| 311 | CLOSED | When Indians attack, the outcome of the attack generally needs a popup. Especially if it's a colony. "wiped out, colonists jubilant" etc. | FIXED. Verified against FUN_5fef_1b0e (viceroy_unpacked_2.c ~91926), where bVar28 means no defending unit at the target tile, so the colony message family and the generic family are mutually exclusive. |
| 312 | CLOSED | Zoom to colony should only show batched popups afterwards that relate to the same colony. Not just any popups. | 313 | CLOSED | Continental Congress elections (as in, new FF joining) popups happen after colony-related popups and that seems out of order. IIRC, DOS has elections happen before. | FIXED. | 314 | REFUTED | Colonies automatically place a road on the colony tile when founded. Check DOS. | NOT A BUG. DOS does not place a road at founding: the founding record init FUN_364b_1ba8 (viceroy_unpacked_2.c:56759) sets only mask bit 0x02 (city occupancy), never road 0x08, and all 12 colonies in a real DOS campaign save (original_saves/dutch-campaign/COLONY05.SAV) carry mask 0x03/0x43 with no 0x08. |
| 315 | CLOSED | After a unit interacts with an Indian village (scouts, armed units, colonists, etc) their remaining movement points should be forfeit. | FIXED. ai_contact_apply_popup_result now zeroes menu_unit->moves_left. The subtlety: DOS's FUN_4d56_4528 tail (viceroy_overlays.asm OVL13:0x4c0a) calls FUN_1000_8b24 only when the dispatch returns 1, so Establish Mission (case 3, returns 2) keeps its movement points, as does Attack Village. |
| 316 | CLOSED | Once again, Indians apparently randomly interact with a scout (ie. it was their move) and there was the popup as if it were the player sending a criminal to learn from the Indians. That's wrong. | FIXED. The passive teach pulse is gone from ai_contact_indian_meet_trade. Root cause: ai_contact_teach_skill was a fandom invention. |
| 317 | CLOSED | Special resources on a tile that is on the edge of fog have their pallettes corrupted. | FIXED. The fog-edge fill used blit_map_sprite_where_dest(..., 0), which fills every colour-0 pixel in the tile, including the black outline pixels inside resource overlays drawn earlier, with neighbour-terrain pixels. |
| 318 | CLOSED | Equipping veteran soldiers with horses in Europe caused them to look like normal (unskilled) dragoons. On the main map they were looking like normal veteran dragoons. These things seem like they shouldn't happen in any reasonably structured code; the unit chrome should be the same, so why would they look different? | FIXED. Two icon rules had drifted apart: units_map_sprite grants veteran art to both veteran professions (0x15 Veteran Soldiers and 0x17 Veteran Dragoons), but europe_dock_sprite in game_loop.c only checked profession == Dragoon, so a horse-equipped Veteran Soldier drew as a plain Dragoon on the dock. |
| 319 | CLOSED | Do Aztecs in DOS ever beg for food? I thought they were the ones to BRING food to colonies that had little. | ANSWERED (yes, they can beg) and FIXED (a real port defect). Begging is not tribe personality; it is the visiting village's own FUN_4d56_2154 terrain and population economics (ask[0] > bid[0] on food, colony food > 74, rng(1,100) <= delta), and its exact mirror is the gift-food arm (bid[0] > ask[0], colony food <= 25, top up to 75). |
| 320 | CLOSED | Abandon colony popup is messed up. Wrong font, no advisor flair, text overflows box. | FIXED. It was a colony-screen-local box (colony_screen_draw_message_popup) drawing message_text with a single unwrapped font_draw_text, no @width and no figure. |
| 321 | CLOSED | European diplomacy popups have the options duplicated in the main popup text. | FIXED by the GAME.TXT blank-line parser rewrite described above; ai_diplo.c's ai_talk_body and ai_talk_choice read the body and the choices from the same section, so both halves were duplicated by the old keyword matcher. |
| 322 | CLOSED | "The Sioux teach outdoor skills" popup on Indians coming to my colony. That's not a real popup. | FIXED, same root cause as the scout teach popup. The string was an OpenCol-invented one with no GAME.TXT tag, and it died with the passive teach pulse. |
| 323 | CLOSED | Tax hike popup claims to raise the tax rate by 1% to 8%, but tax rate is already 1%. Recheck DOS about how those work. | FIXED. Asm 38fd:3fd4 does NOT AX then INC AX (absolute value) and passes it as FUN_281f_09ae(0, |delta|) = NUMBER0, with FUN_281f_09ae(1, tax_rate) = NUMBER1. The port hardcoded tok.number0 = 1; it now uses the applied delta. |
| 324 | CLOSED | Indian visiting colony popups seem strange. In DOS, they visibly wander into the colony (much like a combat bump) and then a popup appears with whatever they wanted. | FIXED. Visits now call units_combat_watch_notify so the visitor performs the combat-style lunge into the colony before the popup appears, mirroring DOS's FUN_281f_0e08/02d0/09ba walk-in. Files: units.c, ai_contact.c. |
| 325 | CLOSED | Indians never seem to visit bearing gifts. This is by far the most common popup one gets from peaceful Indian visitors in DOS. | FIXED. The gift arm existed as dead code with no header declaration and no caller. Wired ai_contact_try_village_gifts (@INDIANGIVEFOOD/@INDIANGIVESTUFF) into ai.c section 9, returning 1 so the beg arm is skipped, since DOS's bVar6 picks exactly one half per encounter. |
| 326 | CLOSED | "Whose turn it is" colored rectangle is drawn over colony UI. It should stay on the sidebar, never to be shown on any other screen. | FIXED. turn_draw_owner_indicator was drawn unconditionally at the end of every render pass mid-EOT; it is now gated off whenever a full-screen view (colony, Europe, report, pedia, atlas, Hall of Fame, exploits) covers the sidebar. File: game_loop.c, render_log_sample. |
| 327 | REFUTED | Should Indians be alarmed by combat units on ships? I'm not sure they do in DOS. | NOT A BUG. DOS never counts them: FUN_4cc6_03f8's 20-tile threat ring skips any tile where FUN_281f_0768 then FUN_13e4_0074 reports terrain class 0x19/0x1a (water), and its stack walk ignores unit types 0xd..0x12 (ships). |
| 328 | CLOSED | "New Spain cancel their treaty with New Netherlands" seems an odd popup, given especially that it was involving me the player, and there was no other interaction. | FIXED, and DOS behaviour now proven. FUN_5bfb_13b0's cancel branch pushes tag 0x1898 = CANCELTREATY, but no file in COLONIZE/ ships that section. |
| 329 | CLOSED | Indian capitals should be indicated by a starburst symbol. I'm sure it is somewhere in the icons file. | FIXED. Confirmed in DOS asm FUN_112b_0790 (112b:09a1-09bf): tribe state bit 0x04 (capital) draws sprite 0x12 one-based = ICONS.SS 17, a 16x16 yellow starburst, centred over the village icon and before the alarm/mission chrome. |
| 330 | CLOSED | There was a stack of 2 dragoons and an artillery. Artillery got chosen despite receiving the -75% penalty for being in the open. Fix the damn defender choice already. | FIXED. DOS FUN_5fef_0000 (OVL17 asm 0x80-0x11c, a block Ghidra's C export mangles) applies a pick-score shift right by 3 to artillery when the tile has no European colony and the artillery is not Fortify/Fortified, with the doubling against Indians applying only on a colony. |
| 331 | CLOSED | I attacked a Spanish colony with a scout and got a popup "natives grow hostile". That's not real. | FIXED. The attack ran ai_diplo_declare_war, which applied a fandom-invented minus 5 to all eight tribes; the resulting sticky rise popped the hostility message. |
| 332 | CLOSED | I attacked a Spanish colony with a scout (defender was a colonist) and won, and captured the colonist, but the scout did not enter the colony nor capture it. If there was only a colonist defending and I won, the colony should have been captured with all hands. | FIXED. The port ran the 465b movement-point overspend roll after combat, so a failed roll captured the colonist but refused the entry that seizes the colony. |
| 333 | CLOSED | Artillery in the open penalty should not apply against Indian villages. In fact, there's a bonus to artillery for that eventuality. | FIXED. DOS 1b0e keys the artillery clauses on FUN_281f_06be, the layer2 & 2 settlement bit, which is set on both colony and village tiles; the port used a colony-only lookup, so artillery attacking a village took the minus 75 percent. |
| 334 | CLOSED | Defending village braves got an "Aztec" bonus at +150%. What is that supposed to be? I suspect it's a real bonus but mislabeled. | FIXED. It is a real bonus with both the wrong label and the wrong maths. DOS 015e's village arm (viceroy 8989-9002) is base times 1.5, times 2 at tech > 1, doubled again for a capital dwelling; the old (probe+1)*2 that produced +150 percent is the COLONY arm's formula misattributed. |
| 335 | CLOSED | When attacking Indian villages, the game freezes until I click the "close application" button (window system UI) upon which it unfreezes. WTF. | FIXED. Deadlock, not a hang: the Attack Village handler ran units_try_move (which calls game_combat_popup_pump) while its own ACTIONS popup result was still unconsumed, and since ai_popup_busy counts has_result the pump spun forever with nothing open to answer and presentation refused, until SDL_QUIT broke it. |
| 336 | CLOSED | Artillery purchased in Europe should go up in price. Starts at 500g, but 100g more for every prior purchase. | FIXED. DOS FUN_38fd_4b50 shows base + (nation+0x1e)*100 for type 0xb and does price += count*100 then count++ on purchase; the counter is the already-mapped but unwired nation.artillery_count. |
| 337 | CLOSED | Sometimes units with Fortify order (not Fortified) become active/controlled. They should not be in the control queue at all. | FIXED. `units_orders_skip_turn` deliberately left FORTIFY (5) out on the grounds that digging in already zeroes the allotment. |
| 338 | CLOSED | Pretty sure dragoons should have their entire movement spent from stepping off a ship onto land. Check DOS. | FIXED, and it is not dragoon-specific. DOS FUN_465b_0000 at LAB_465b_05ca (annotated `ai/move_spent.c` section 4) adds the step cost and then, when the move CROSSES the water/land boundary with no European settlement on either tile, sets moves_spent to the unit's maximum. |
| 339 | CLOSED | Sometimes, buy project in multipurpose-construction popup in colony UI produces popup that is invisible until I leave colony UI. It should be visible over colony UI, and a blocker until I deal with it. It should not wait until I exit colony UI to be displayed. | FIXED, and the "sometimes" is the tell: the colony branch of the renderer does draw an OPEN popup, but game_update's queue pump is suppressed while `colony_zoom_popup_hold` is set — the flag that keeps the rest of the end-of-turn message batch from interrupting a colony the player elected to zoom into. |
| 340 | CLOSED | Tax hike popup is still inconsistent with game state. When it occurs, it should list you in the text what the tax increase proposed is, what it would be in total if you accept, and only apply the change after you accept. For instance, if it says "raised by 6%" and "the tax rate is now 11%" that means that it is a proposal to raise the tax rate from 5% to 11%, it's just royally worded as if you have no choice, but you do have a choice. | FIXED. The two @KINGTAX numbers were already right (NUMBER0 = applied delta, NUMBER1 = the total), but the port followed DOS 3dc8 literally and *wrote* the raise before showing the dialog, so the Europe screen, the status line and the save all carried a rate the player had not agreed to while the popup was still asking; Refuse then reverted it. |

It just repeats the popup. | FIXED: answering @HALF with "Charge!" re-entered game_try_unit_move, but unlike the WHACK confirm (sets the asked-once bit) or Break Treaty (opens the war) nothing recorded the answer, so the tired-attack confirm fired again — forever. |
| 406 | CLOSED | Leader name defaults should be appropriate per nation. I was playing Netherlands, but my leader had the default name from the English. | FIXED: the per-nation seeding (NAMES.TXT @LEADERNAME row = nation) only ran in the new-game wizard; game init defaults leader_name to "Walter Raleigh" and the LOAD path never replaced it, so any loaded campaign's exploits screen / Hall of Fame entry / declaration signature carried the English default regardless of nation. |
| 407 | CLOSED | Foreign intervention isn't happening even though I've gathered the required bells. | FIXED — two compounding gates made it unreachable: (a) ai_king_spend_woi_bell_pool routed the bells to ANOTHER REF WAVE whenever the expeditionary pools were nonzero (and blocked entirely on ref_present), and (b) ai_king_foreign_intervene itself returned while the exp pools held anything. |
| 408 | CLOSED | Teaching may not be properly cleared once you remove teachers from the schoolhouse/college/university. Just opened a colony where I previously unassigned all the veteran soldier teachers and turns out all the free colonists were now veteran soldiers. | FIXED — the teachers were being silently re-seated: unassigning left them idle, and the bug-262 EOT idle sweep (colonies_auto_assign_idle) reassigns idle colonists Town-Hall-first but, with Town Hall full (3-worker cap) or absent, its fallback loop walked every building and colonies_assign_workplace accepted the veterans back into the school (they pass the may-teach/tier gates), so teaching resumed invisibly. |
| 409 | CLOSED | Allied intervention force landings need to be shown happening (popup, then MoW depositing units ashore). Also they shouldn't arrive on an inland lake — just had a Man-O-War in a lake deposit units into the lake colony. | FIXED both. VISIBILITY: the popup+slides sequence was already coded (@INTERVENTION/@INTERVENE OKs pump-blocked first, troops spawn on the ship tile and slide into the colony via the move watch, like the REF landing) — but game_move_watch never recentres the view for OWN-nation units, and the intervention force is player-owned, so when the landing colony was off-screen the whole deposit played invisibly. |
| 410 | CLOSED | Custom house sales should be logged in the debug log. | FIXED — `europe_custom_house_autosell_ex` moved goods and gold with no log line at all. It now writes one `EUROPE customs <colony> sold <n> <cargo>: bid/tax/paid/proceeds (nation=…)` per cargo plus an `EUROPE customs <colony> total …$` summary, for AI colonies as well as the human's. The AI warehouse dump-sell (`europe_ai_colony_dump_sell`) got a matching `EUROPE dump-sell` total. |
| 411 | CLOSED | European sales should be logged in the debug log. | FIXED — harbour and unit-hold sales/buys were already logged (`europe_sell_hold`, `europe_sell_hold_partial`, `europe_sell_unit_hold`, `europe_buy_cargo`, `europe_buy_unit_cargo`); the gaps were every Europe money move that does not go through a ship hold. Added: dock `@ARMOPTIONS` arm/equip rows (`EUROPE dock <name>: buy muskets … gold ±n`), treasure cash-in (`EUROPE treasure cashed …`, value / Crown share / credited), back-tax boycott lift (`EUROPE paid …$ back taxes`), plus the Custom House and dump-sell lines above. |
| 412 | CLOSED | Recruitment/purchase/training in Europe should be logged in the debug log. | FIXED — the paid actions were already logged (`EUROPE recruited/trained/purchased` in `europe_recruit_from_pool` / `europe_train` / `europe_purchase`). Missing was the free arrival: a crosses-driven immigrant reaching the docks now logs `EUROPE immigrant on the docks: <name> (crosses)` from `turn_notify_dock_immigrant`. The RECRUIT/TRAIN/PURCHASE dialogs themselves also log their row list and the picked row (see the popup row). |
| 413 | CLOSED | Debug logs about popups should list what the user chose. Can be a log entry after the initial log about the popup and the choices the user had. | FIXED for the dialogs that were missing it. The `AiPopup` queue already emitted `POPUP show … choices=…` followed by `POPUP answered … picked id=… choice="…"` (`ai_popup_log_present` / `ai_popup_finish`), but the Europe list dialogs are a separate widget and logged nothing. `europe_menu_open` now writes `POPUP show tag=EUROPE_<RECRUIT/TRAIN/PURCHASE/DOCK> kind=choice gold=… choices=[1]…` and the confirm/close paths write `POPUP answered tag=EUROPE_<menu> picked row=N` or `cancelled` — a new `menu_answered` flag keeps an Esc/click-away cancel from being reported twice. The @HOWMUCHn amount dialog (Europe buy/sell quantity, colony load/unload) logs the same pair under `tag=HOWMUCH_<kind>` with the amount picked. |
| 414 | CLOSED | Aztec city icon is incorrect in sidebar. Inca too. | FIXED — a C operator bug in the sidebar's native-settlement row (`map_panel.c`): `tech = ... ? (col1 && col1->indian[n].tech) : 0` is a **logical** AND, so any nonzero tech collapsed to 1. Aztec (tech 2) and Inca (tech 3) therefore drew ICONS.SS #11 (Village) and read the Village `@LEVELS` row; tech-0 and tech-1 tribes came out right by accident, which is why only those two looked wrong. The world-map path (`map_panel_render_tribes_on_map`) casts properly, so the map icons were already correct. DOS `FUN_112b_0790` (asm `112b:07f0`) clamps tech to 3 and blits `11 + tech` 1-based = ICONS.SS #10–13, and the sidebar reaches it through `FUN_281f_02b2(…, 100, …)` from `FUN_49dd_0424` — so the port's base index was right, only the value fed in was wrong. Two further parts of the same DOS row were also missing: the capital starburst (ICONS.SS #17, drawn on the fall-through at `112b:09a1` at every scale, not just the map) and the `@LEVELS` row override at `49dd:115b` — a capital forces row 4, so DOS prints "Aztec Capital", not "Aztec City". `docs/combat.md`'s claim that `112b_0790` reads a `DS:0x84c` per-tech table was wrong and is corrected: the icon index comes from `DS:0x5ad8` stride `0x4e`, and `DS:0x84c` is per-tribe relation-bar colour. |
| 415 | CLOSED | Iroquis tribe chief sprite has some flat grey bits where other colors should be. Pallette messed up? | FIXED. Popup art sheets own DAC slots 152..251 (DOS `FUN_1c2e_0022` is always called with AX=0x98/DX=0x64 and overwrites the block unconditionally), but the port only lent the art sheet the slots the host palette left black. `TERRAIN.SS` carries one stray non-black entry inside that block — index 209, grey (113,113,113), unused by any map art — so every popup sheet painting with index 209 showed grey; the Iroquois chief uses it for ~130 px of dark-brown (44,20,16) hair. `ai_popup_sheet_palette_merge` now copies 152..251 outright and keeps the fill-if-black rule outside it, fixing all 32 chief portraits plus the KING/MSS/MYR/SCORE sheets. |
| 416 | CLOSED | I've apparently met with the Aztec tribe despite never coming into proximity to them. I was playing the French. Could another nation have met them, and I got their popup? | FIXED — and it was not another nation's popup. The `layer3` owner nibble is a *stamp*: DOS `FUN_1427_02ca` repaints it on every step and leaves it behind, which is safe there only because a unit never stands on a settlement tile it doesn't own. The port's Brave walker can — `ai_native_pick_dir_asm`'s `foreign_euro_pull` exception lets it step onto a Euro colony tile — and the step tail stamped that tile with the tribe's nation while the settlement bit stayed set, so `ai_contact_encounter_scan` (the `FUN_5bfb_3180` port, which reads exactly that nibble on `layer2 & 2` tiles) then opened first contact with that tribe for any Euro unit that later stepped beside its own colony. The scan now resolves a settlement tile's owner from the real `col1->tribe[]` record, and native movers no longer repaint settlement tiles. Fixed alongside: the meet tune and woodcut were keyed on the Col1 nation id 4..11 instead of DOS's tribe slot `*(int *)0x8d52`, so "THE AZTEC EMPIRE" / "THE INCA NATION" and the Cuzco/Tenochtitlan tunes were unreachable. |
| 417 | CLOSED | Indians came to demand Horses very damn quickly, at zero apparent alarm. | FIXED. DOS's `FUN_5bfb_022e` picks gift-or-demand from one `bVar6` mood decision per encounter, and at alarm 0 that decision is always "generous"; the port's three reconstructed arms (gift/beg/reparations) each had their own 8-turn throttle, so the turn after a village handed over a gift the gift arm bailed on its own cooldown (with `contact_state` freshly cleared by the midpass) and the demand arm fired at zero alarm. All three arms now share one per-nation visit throttle, restoring DOS's "one resolved encounter". 2026-09-10 follow-up: the shared 8-turn throttle itself was invented (DOS `FUN_5bfb_022e` and its move-tail caller have no turn counter — pacing is the walk-up trigger, the per-turn `contact_state` clear in `FUN_4d56_1b3a`, the alarm ceiling and the mood RNG, all ported); removed. The gift-before-demand arm order plus the `contact_state` latch carry the mutual exclusion that actually fixed this bug. |
| 418 | CLOSED | When I go-to to an Indian settlement, the go-to shouldn't end on the adjacent tile. It should be interpreted as though I actually wanted to move into it, and it should bring up the appropriate dialogs. | FIXED. The pacer stopped one tile short of an ordered village and handed control back (a leftover of the earlier "Village ahead — awaiting orders" guard); DOS runs every goto step through `FUN_465b_0000`, so the final step is a move command into the dwelling. `units_goto_dest_is_village_entry` now detects the arrival frame and `game_loop` dispatches that step through `game_try_unit_move`, raising the same woodcut 7 + `@ACTIONS` flow (or the unmet-tribe warn) an arrow-key step raises; the order is cleared before the dispatch so a peaceful meet-from-adjacent does not re-fire the menu. |
| 419 | CLOSED | Merchantman chrome is messed up in Europe (orders box misplaced). | FIXED — the Europe harbor renderer passed `EuropeHarborShip.type_index` straight to `unit_chrome_corner_for_type`, but `europe_purchase()` files a newly bought hull with `type_index = -1`, so the type missed DOS's `0x0d..0x12` ship range and the orders box fell through to the BOTTOM_RIGHT corner instead of TOP_LEFT. The chrome now resolves the display type through the same by-name fallback the sprite lookup already used (`europe_ship_display_type`, game_loop.c). Verified against DOS `FUN_112b_01ba` (Caravel/Merchantman TOP_LEFT, Galleon..Man-O-War TOP_RIGHT) and the original Europe screenshot's 18×18 cell with its box at the icon origin. |
| 420 | CLOSED | Do unskilled missionaries have the skilled missionary sprite? | WAS A BUG, FIXED. DOS `FUN_112b_0060` downgrades a commissioned missionary whose colonist is not a Jesuit (profession != 0x18) to icon 0x4e = sprite 77, the pale book-carrying pose, while the Jesuit keeps @UNIT icon 106 = sprite 105 — the same expert/generic split Pioneers, Soldiers, Scouts and Dragoons get. The port had no missionary branch, so unskilled missionaries did wear the Jesuit sprite, on the map and on the Europe dock. Fixed in `units_map_sprite` and `europe_dock_sprite`; the same reading of `FUN_112b_0002` also corrected the Jesuit *working-colonist* portrait (77 → 61, matching labor.png) and gave Expert Teachers their own sprite 99 instead of the Free Colonist fallback. |
| 421 | CLOSED | Newly bought Merchantman sent to the New World spawned in an unexplored sea lane tile, and did not even insta-reveal the fog. I had to move it to reveal. | FIXED. `game_europe_deliver_bound_ships` spawned the arriving ship without ever running the sight/explored update, so a Europe arrival landed inside its own fog; DOS `FUN_48d3_048e` ends with `FUN_281f_07a0` (= `FUN_13f1_02f8`, the same reveal a move runs) unconditionally. The arrival now runs `game_reveal_sight_for_unit`, and the arrival tile is picked by the DOS ring walk `units_spiral_place_hs_near` (48d3_048e/0434: high seas, empty-or-own-nation) instead of a nearest-tile sweep that refused our own ships. The AI's Europe-exit teleport got the same reveal. |
| 422 | CLOSED | Trade/haggle dialog is missing the tribe chief flair. | FIXED. Every dialog in DOS `FUN_4d56_2820` is a `FUN_291f_019c(tag, *(0x8d52))` call, which arms `DS:0x1f5c` so `FUN_6f74_0042` draws the `IND{tribe}A{tier}.SS` chief; the port's four trade CHOICE popups (hold pick, @TRADE0/1, @BUYWHICH, @BUY0/1) were enqueued without the portrait side-channel. Added `ai_contact_chief_flair()` and called it after each enqueue. |
| 423 | CLOSED | A unit with spent moves should not be land-fall-able. It should stay on the ship. | FIXED. `FUN_4720_015c` offers landfall only to cargo whose spent byte is below its max MP, and walking aboard from open shore is the 465b_05ca force-to-max — so a unit that used its turn stays on the ship, and with no eligible passenger DOS refuses the move outright instead of raising @LANDFALL. The port could not distinguish boarding's park-at-zero from a real spend; a new `mp_spent_turn` marker (set on shore boarding, cleared by the day-top refresh) now gates the picker, the offer, the unload and the wake refund, while a parked-but-fresh (in-port loaded / sentry) passenger still lands normally. |
| 424 | CLOSED | Popups about rival monarchs considering granting their colonies independence show up way too early. Like in 1530. This is far earlier than I've seen them in DOS. | FIXED. `ai_king_sol_percent` (port of `FUN_43f7_0004`) fell back to `liberty_bells_total / 4` whenever a colony had no rebel divisor or a nation had no colony records — but that field is a lifetime bell total that runs into the millions, so it clamped to 100% rebel sentiment, and the year-end Section D reads `rebel_sentiment × census_pop_proxy / 100` as the rival's rebel count, firing @OTHERMIGHT as soon as the census passed the band floor. DOS's `FUN_15eb_0274` returns 0 with no accumulation and `FUN_43f7_0004` has no fallback at all; both stand-ins are deleted (the same one was already removed from `colony_prod_sol_percent`). Calibrated against shipped saves: 1505/1550 fixtures give v=0, the 1680 save lands inside the band — the popup belongs to a mature empire. |
| 425 | CLOSED | The sales lines in the top bar, both for custom house and European Status, should be sped up. 3x as fast, please. | FIXED — status-strip sale lines now display 3× faster than DOS: 166 ms while more lines are queued behind, 657 ms for the last of a run (was 500 / 1971). Scoped to DOS arm kinds 1/2, the sale arms used by both the Custom House autosell run and the European Status sell lines, so any other status-line use keeps DOS's own dwell. |
| 426 | REFUTED | "The <nation> settlers in <colony> have committed intolerable acts" popup needs tribe chief flair. They also appear to ask for half the colony cargo, rather than all of it like in DOS. | PARTLY FIXED. The chief portrait was missing for the same reason as the trade-dialog row (DOS raw 96882/96959 show @INDIANCITY/@INDIANWAGONS are `019c` chief audiences) and is now wired. The demand amount is NOT a bug: DOS `FUN_5bfb_022e` (raw 96843-96866, asm 5bfb:0c1c) demands `min(colony_stock, 100)` and halves it only when `rng(0, difficulty+1)` rolls 0 — 1 visit in 2 at Discoverer, 1 in 6 at Viceroy — which the port already reproduces exactly; a warehouse colony holding 200 also reads as "half" purely from the 100 cap. |
| 427 | CLOSED | Contact popups with the Tupi got interrupted by contact popups with the Spanish. These should not mix. Player has to deal with one event chain, then the other one can start. | FIXED — contact chains now present atomically. DOS runs a contact exchange as one blocking inline call (`FUN_5bfb_3180` → `FUN_5bfb_022e` / `FUN_5bfb_153e`), so a Tupi meet and a Spanish encounter are strictly sequential; the port's queue is async and each chain enqueues its next dialog only when the previous one is answered, so the follow-up landed behind whatever the same pulse had queued. Every popup in the contact/153e family now carries a chain key derived from (Euro nation, other party), and the presenter holds that key — analogous to the colony-zoom hold from the zoom-ordering row — until nothing in the queue carries it any more. |
| 428 | CLOSED | On the Indian report, tribe names and tribe advancement use the wrong font. Those should be FONTTINY. | FIXED. Tribe name and advancement word now draw in FONTTINY like every other line on the screen — the whole `3f41` report overlay only ever touches the FONTTINY font pointer `DS:0x89e` (`DS:0x268a`/FONTINTR is referenced zero times there), and the Indian Adviser reaches text only through the FONTTINY thunks `FUN_281f_0100/0114/013c`. The FONTINTR slot the earlier screenshot-driven pass added is gone, and the name→stats gap is now derived the way DOS derives it at `3f41:02dc` (FONTTINY header height + 2 = 8, not the hardcoded 9) — which also matches the golden `indian.png` to the pixel. |
| 429 | CLOSED | Playing on the original Americas map, the western edge is apparently cut off one tile too soon. That's where the sea lanes are for the west coast. | FIXED (different cause than diagnosed). The viewport was never clipped — the port already draws columns 1..56 exactly like DOS (`FUN_6ba1_000c` clamps origin to `[1, map_w-cols-1]`; west sea lane is column 1). What was wrong is that `units_enter_probe` used raw array bounds instead of DOS's `FUN_137f_000a` inset bound (`x < 1 || map_w-1 <= x`), so a ship could step off the west sea lane onto the unplayable rim column 0 — a tile the view never scrolls to and no click can address, which reads as the map ending one tile early. The rim is now denied with `BLOCKED_EDGE` on all four sides. |
| 430 | REFUTED | In a recent save, Montreal seems to lack a carpenter's house. Clicking it says "build it first". That's incorrect. It's one of the buildings all colonies have at start. See: missing_carpenters.SAV | FIXED — the save was never broken. Montreal's Carpenter's Shop bit is set and imports correctly; the colony-screen hit test was the fault. DOS's `FUN_2f2b_44d4` scans slot positions 0..14, tests the size-class box, and *keeps scanning past* a slot whose category is unbuilt, so a placeholder can never answer a click; the port scanned categories instead and let the unbuilt Docks category's coast placeholder — the one slot pair that overlaps, sitting on top of slot 8 where the Carpenter's Shop lives — swallow the click and report "Build it first". The scan now matches DOS exactly (slot order, class box, no per-pixel test, unbuilt slots skipped, no-match does nothing), and a regression test sweeps the whole viewport for both halves of that invariant. |
| 431 | CLOSED | The option to clear specialty is missing. In DOS, it's one of the options available on the choice of workplace popup in the colony UI, for a specialist colonist. | FIXED. Ported FUN_2f2b_348c row 0x61: the field-jobs popup gets a "Clear Specialty" row for any specialist colonist (profession outside FUN_15eb_0002's ordinary set 0x13/0x19/0x1a/0x1b/0x1c); picking it asks GAME.TXT @LOBOTOMIZE (DS:0xcae) with the specialty name, and Yes writes profession 0x1c (Free Colonist) exactly as FUN_281f_0cae does. Mouse and Enter paths both. |
| 432 | CLOSED | Colony UI, transport view. Dragging cargo from the hold of one transport onto the sprite of another transport in the same location should move the cargo to the target's hold, if any are available. | FIXED. Dropping a hold's cargo onto ANOTHER docked transport's sprite now moves it ship-to-ship (units_load_goods into the target, source hold cleared, partial fit returned to the source), never through the warehouse; a full target reports "No empty hold". |
| 433 | CLOSED | Spoilage warning popup in colony UI has messed up interpolations. First number should be how much is already there, second number is OK, third number is how much you just deposited. | FIXED. @WAREHOUSEFULL's numbers now match the template: NUMBER0 = stock BEFORE the deposit (the transfer's moved amount is backed out when it already landed), NUMBER1 = capacity, NUMBER2 = the deposit itself (was never set, and NUMBER0 included the deposit). All four unload paths (drag, U key, unload-all, HOWMUCH amount entry) pass the amounts. |
| 434 | CLOSED | Almost certain new founding fathers being elected should happen at the start of the player's turn, not just after you click end turn. But check DOS. | FIXED, and DOS agrees: FUN_4345_0a22 runs inside each nation's own FUN_3844_00f2 pass, and 130d runs the human's 00f2 immediately before their Move Pieces. The human's election check moved out of founding_fathers_tick (SETUP) into TURN_PROC_FINISH (founding_fathers_tick_human_elect) — the Congress debate now opens at the start of your turn with the production chrome. AI nations still elect in SETUP. |
| 435 | REFUTED | Indian alarm seems almost non-functional. Even as French with Pocahontas, I would expect nearby Indians to be at least minimally alarmed and show the ! icons on their settlements. I see very little of that. | INVESTIGATED, not changed — the port is term-for-term equal to DOS FUN_4cc6_03f8 (checked against the decompile: base formula, building weights by difficulty, distance divisor, off-continent >>1, military-pressure ring, mission adjustments) and the render (112b_0790) draws marks only when that live threat score is > 0, tiered by the settlement attitude word. French colonies halve the score (uVar5==1 branch) and Pocahontas halves it AGAIN (FUN_281f_07b4(nation,0x10)) — DOS-real, so a French+Pocahontas game genuinely quarters every village's threat and small colonies floor to 0: few marks is the DOS outcome. Headless probe over campaign saves confirmed the math. |
| 436 | CLOSED | Construction popup font should be green. Also, the hammers/tools cost should be aligned to the right. There should be no need for multiple columns, either. Check DOS. | FIXED. Green ink (index 10) for title and rows; costs ("N Hammers M Tools", DOS 2f2b_5a68's words) right-aligned at the row edge (the DS:0xd1d "|   " tab); and the two-column layout removed — DOS 2f2b_5bd2 never columns, past 0x16=22 rows it PAGES 16 rows at a time chained with a More... row, which the popup now does (click More to advance, selection follows pages on keyboard). |
| 437 | CLOSED | Pretty sure players should always be asked if they want to attack an Euro colony, even if it is unarmed. | FIXED. A combat unit stepping onto a foreign Euro COLONY now always gets a confirm ("Shall we attack the colony of {X}?", Attack/cancel) — armed or not, at war or not — before the move commits (AI_POPUP_TAG_COLONY_ATTACK, one-shot latch like @HALF's so the Yes retry doesn't re-ask; the @HAVETREATY break-treaty retry sets the latch too, so no double prompt). |
| 438 | CLOSED | Moving a scout into a foreign colony should bring up the scout-appropriate menu of what to do there. They aren't just military units; they have other abilities. | FIXED. Ported FUN_5f7a_000e: a Scout stepping onto a foreign Euro colony now gets GAME.TXT @SCOUTCOLONY (Meet With Mayor / Infiltrate Colony / Attack Colony / Nothing). Meet = the 5bfb_153e encounter dialog (WoI: @NOMAYORSDURINGREV refuses); Infiltrate = DOS roll — threshold (Stockade-chain count + 6)*2, halved for a Seasoned Scout (profession 0x16), RNG(1,0x24) must beat it — success opens a view of the colony, failure is @LOSTOURSCOUTS, the scouts despawn and the colony stables gain their 100 horses (+0xaa); Attack rejoins the normal attack path (row 443's confirm counts as answered). |
| 439 | CLOSED | I refused Indian demands to hand over goods, and they didn't attack. In DOS they do that if you refuse. | FIXED. The refusal itself was already DOS-right (LAB_5bfb_0ff2: village attitude word +0x80) — but the native attack gate (units_at_war_for_move) only tested the war bit and nation alarm >= 0x4b, so the grudge never turned into an attack. A native mover whose HOME village holds an attitude word over 0x7f toward that European now opens combat — the same 0x7f threshold DOS's own FUN_521d_0906 uses on DS:0x54f6. |
| 440 | CLOSED | Fair sure the "wagon wheels" sound effect should be played only when a wagon train arrives at a colony, not every step it takes. | FIXED. Event 0x52 (COLDIG 12 wagon wheels) now plays only when the Wagon Train's step lands on a colony tile, not on every overland step. |
| 441 | CLOSED | Incas, who are in their historical South American spot, are demanding stuff from me. I accept that their brave just wandered over, but I have no colonies over there. They should not have any cause to demand stuff from me. I refused, and they didn't attack my wagon train next turn. | FIXED. Two holes. (1) FUN_5bfb_022e is ONE encounter that picks a half off `bVar6` (`bVar6 = local_10 == 0 && bVar5`, viceroy 96707-96731; the demand half at LAB_5bfb_0def is guarded by `if (!bVar6)`), but the port split the halves into two functions and only the gift half evaluated it. @INDIANWAGONS needs no colony, so a Brave wandering up to a lone Wagon Train made the gift arm bail early (no colony anywhere near) and the demand arm then ran with no mood gate at all. Both flavors now run DOS's entry mood — the hostile `local_10` roll (rng(1,0x80) vs word-0x80) and `bVar5` (max(0, alarm-0x19)*4 + village word <= rng(1,0x148)) — drawn once per encounter and shared with the gift arm. At alarm 0 with a clean village word bVar5 is always true, so a grievance-free tribe can no longer demand anything. (2) The demand arm bound the visitor's village with a "first village of the nation" fallback; DOS aborts the whole visit when the unit's origin byte is unset (`if (*(char *)(param_3 * 0x1c + 0x314a) < '\0') goto LAB_5bfb_1005`), and the fallback could land the refusal's +0x80 on a village that is not the demanding Brave's home, where units_native_village_grudge (the mover's own +0x314a row, as FUN_521d_0906 reads DS:0x54f6) never sees it. Fallback removed. Also: the grudge-carrying Brave used to burn its whole allotment in the quiet 14fe wander before §9's raid pass, which needs remaining MP — an alarmed Brave (home village word > 0x7f) standing next to the offender now holds still for the raid dispatch instead (021a's alarmed branch is PARKED; this is its stand-in) and is not diverted into the escort/follow arm. |
| 442 | CLOSED | Iroquis attacked my colony, and then gave me gifts in the same interaction. Pretty sure only one of these should occur. Doubly when they have next to no alarm against me. I think their braves survived; I didn't get any popups about damage or deaths or stolen goods. Then they kept attacking my colony several turns in a row, also apparently winning, but not doing any damage to the colony. | FIXED, three defects. (1) DOS's move tail (FUN_5bfb_35xx, viceroy 98628-98690) walks the stepped-onto tile ONCE and picks a single limb off the war test — hostile, or the FUN_5bfb_022e visit (gift/beg/demand) latched per nation in `aiStack_20`. The port splits those limbs across two passes in ai.c §9, so the SAME Brave could resolve a gift and then be picked up again by the raid pulse; a per-Brave per-turn latch now retires it after the peaceful visit. (2) The raid chrome had the @INDIANWAR / @INDIANSURPRISE lines as arms INSIDE the outcome chain, so any raid by a tribe not yet at war printed only "their chief denies involvement" while the loot was applied silently — no stolen-goods, burn or gold line at all. DOS FUN_5fef_0f14 always fires the per-kind tag for a human victim (0x1b94 @RAIDSTORES / 0x1b9f @RAIDBURN / 0x1ba8 @RAIDSHIP / 0x1bb1 @RAIDGOLD / 0x1bba @RAIDNOTHING, raw 99909-100020); the war/deniability sentence now rides in front of that line instead of replacing it. (3) The raiding party survived. DOS's only caller of 0f14 is FUN_5fef_1b0e's loser limb (raw 101142) — the native attacker is already dead when the raid resolver runs (@RAIDNOTHING says so: "raiding party wiped out"). The port's pulse reaches the resolver with no combat, so it now discharges the raider itself; the Brave used to stay parked on the colony tile and re-raid it every turn, forever, at no risk. |
