| 445 | REFUTED | I don't think I've ever seen Indians offer me horses as gifts in DOS. | NOT REPRODUCED statically (2026-09-15). @INDIANGIVESTUFF pick is DOS-literal: sort 2154 bid[] ascending (FUN_1cf8_000a), take rank rng(1,3) from the top, skip food and silver(tech<2); horses are not excluded in DOS either (viceroy_unpacked_2.c 87908-87921). bid[8] = horse_breeding/(difficulty/2+1) — zero until a tribe acquires horses (sale, @INDIANCITY accept); every port_saves/*.SAV has horse_herds = horse_breeding = 0 for all tribes, so bid[8] = 0 there. Ties among equal keys rank by index, so a colony whose horse stock is near warehouse capacity (key forced to 1) can be gifted horses when all other bids are 0/1 — same in DOS. If it recurs, please save the game the turn it happens. |

Not a renderer bug: AMER2.MP carries 146 hill/mountain tiles with forest classes, and DOS runs the FUN_684c_08c0 tail on a loaded map too (asm 684c:08e2 → LAB_684c_17f1): HS outline rings, arctic rows 0/h−1, bit-4 forest demote, hills/mountains lose forest (typ & 7). The port only ran that pass inside map_generate; it is now `map_gen_finalize_border_and_forest`, also applied on the Original Americas new-game load. All 57 DOS saves on generated maps have 0 hill+forest tiles, consistent. |
| 447 | CLOSED | Adding a colonist to a colony doesn't recalculate the amount of SoL membership percentage instantly in the colony UI. It should. | FIXED. Docs: sons_of_liberty.md. |

Speak-with-chief (thunk_FUN_1000_a60c, overlays 77530-77615) matches the port line for line: first visit only (village +3 bit 8 latch, persisted in col1), gated `alarm < rng(0, seasoned?140:100)`, then rng(1,3): 1 → guides (a Seasoned Scout rolling 1 falls through to tales), 2 → tales, 3 → beads. So tales = 1/3 of first visits for a plain Scout, 2/3 for a Seasoned one — the Seasoned case is likely what feels frequent. No other port path emits @CHIEFAREA. |
| 449 | CLOSED | AI Euro ships seem to be going around in loops. They used to wiggle between two tiles, you fixed that, but now they're still going in circles. Three causes in ai_euro.c: (1) every Europe exit set the opening west-explore goto (4,y) even mid-game — greedy ocean steps went west into a land pocket, the pathfinder fallback routed back east, net zero per turn; now an established nation's exit goto is coastal water by the passenger's colony / nearest own coastal colony. (2) Greedy-then-pathfinder each act undid itself; the stall now latches the pathfinder for that goto (`s_euro_ship_route_latch`, cleared on any goto write). (3) The thin Europe-export arm sailed with any export cargo, so a Caravel round-tripped Europe every 4 turns with 2–8 furs; it now needs ≥50 aboard (its own stock>99/leave-50 load rule). Follow-up (same day): the thin "nearest foe ship / enemy port" naval hunt and the Galleon "war transport target" were port inventions — DOS has no distant naval hunt. Ported the real thing: raw 90210-90219 sends an idle hull (or a busy one with a foreign unit adjacent) into the shared 8-direction scorer LAB_521d_4d2e, whose ship arms (open-sea step, far-roam latch raw 88632, fort/Artillery term × holds, west lean, LAB_52aa odds attack — attack-0 hulls skip foe tiles) are now in `ai_euro_20e6_wander_step` / `_ship_far_roam` / `_ship_wander_act`. Idle AI ships roam as in DOS instead of parking; warships fight what the pick puts next to them. Also guarded the opening-only "post-found cruise tip" arm (one colony, water tip) which had yanked a mid-game hull onto a land tile. 61/61, goldens byte-green, no cycles on any campaign save. |

(1) `s_units_goto_last_dir` (FUN_6662_0f74 anti-backtrack shadow) was zero-initialised and read as "last step = North", and the AI ship sail loop never recorded its own `units_try_move` steps into it; a far goal whose first flood/waypoint step was South was rejected as a reversal, the greedy tier took over and ping-ponged (32,51)↔(31,50) forever. (2) The sail loop refused ANY occupied tile for both the greedy and the pathfinder step; two own ships whose routes crossed each other blocked each other indefinitely. What the row did expose is a save-interop defect, now fixed in `col1_bridge.c`: the port wrote its Europe harbor at `228+n` and Bound at `232+n`, so a real DOS westbound stack one tick from landing (lategame `COLONY06.SAV`: Dutch hulls at 231 with counter 0, chained Artillery and goto (53,56), vs the French at 233 with counter 1) imported as a docked ship and re-exported one lane too far out. Lanes are now `236+n` harbor / `228+n` Bound (counter 0 kept verbatim = "arrives next tick") / `244+n` Expected, which also resolves the old "unresolved fifth family" at `240+n` in save_format_map.md. |

| 528 | CLOSED | AI turn-top act-state clear (`FUN_521d_0a60` raw 87560) is ported for values >= 10 only: DOS's 1/2/3 arm collides with the port's SENTRY landfall latch, which overloads the same `orders` value (`src/core/ai_euro.c`). Moved the port's own first-colony landfall goto memory off `orders == SENTRY` onto a new port-only `ColonizeUnit.ai_landfall_wait` bool (`src/core/units.h`, not saved — COLNXEXT does not carry it), set alongside `orders = UNITS_ORDER_SENTRY` at all 8 AI-unload/landfall sites in `ai_euro.c` (3 direct `ai_euro_set_goto` calls, 5 via `ai_euro_unload_pax_at`) and cleared by any non-SENTRY `ai_euro_set_goto` call. Hit one real follow-on bug during verification: `units_wake` (`units.c`) decides whether to refund a unit's overnight-park MP by reading `prev == orders` at wake time, but by the time a landfall-waiting unit reaches its first-colony-land wake gate this turn, the new unconditional 0a60 clear has already zeroed `orders` to NONE, so the SENTRY-based park check no longer fired and TURN3→4 lost a unit's walk (stuck unmoved, MP fully but wrongly spent) — fixed by also treating `prev == NONE && ai_landfall_wait` as parked (still gated on the real `park_nights` counter so only an overnight park refunds, not same-turn spend). Since each golden TURN step reloads a fresh `.SAV` and the flag is not saved, col1 import (`col1_bridge.c`) now reconstructs it as `orders == SENTRY` at load time — a safe over-approximation matching this bug's own premise. `make test` 67/67, `golden_ai_turns` 6/6, `make golden` clean. |

| 544 | CLOSED | Pioneer that stepped onto a ship from land, and was then carried by that ship into a colony on the same turn, still had moves available. It should not. | FIXED 2026-09-21: DOS-checked. Walking aboard from open shore sets the passenger's spent byte +0x3149 to max (465b_05ca, #423), and nothing on the ship-move or dock path rewrites a passenger's +0x3149 (its only writers are the new-turn reset `FUN_130d_0290`, spawns and AI arms), so in DOS the unit lands in the colony spent. The port refilled the allotment on docking: `units_disembark_all` and the dock arm of `units_unload_passenger_w` treated every zero as a boarding park. Aligned fully to DOS the same day: a passenger now carries its own allotment in the hold (`ColonizeUnit.aboard_moves`, captured at boarding, reset at the day top), and waking, docking and unloading hand back exactly that. A shore crossing carries 0; boarding from a colony tile charges the water step like 465b's ADD; an overnight Sentry/Fortified park carries the full allotment. The Col1 export writes the carried spent byte for passengers instead of a flat 0. Regression in `test_units` (dock-spent: spent lands 0, partly spent lands its remainder). |
| 545 | CLOSED | Moving next to a previously uncontacted European power (particularly if encountered none before) should have an effect. IIRC DOS opens up initial diplomacy right there. | FIXED 2026-09-21: the first-contact audience (FUN_5bfb_153e: woodcut 10 + @HELLOFIRST/@HELLOAHOY, then the talk; met bit 0x20 set both ways on return, per FUN_5bfb_3180 raw 98457-98713) was already ported, but the human-move trigger only looked at adjacent units, skipped ships entirely and ignored whether tiles were land. It now follows DOS: the neighbour's owner is the unit's nation, or else the settlement owner (FUN_281f_07e0 / 06be), so an ungarrisoned foreign colony also triggers first contact. The mover's tile and the neighbour tile must both be land (FUN_281f_0768), so a ship in a colony qualifies and one at sea does not; the AI-move trigger got the same land checks. The greeting tokens replace invented English literals with DOS's raw 97696-97704 values: difficulty title + leader name, the target's country name, and @GREATKINGS / @GREATDEEDS for the target. AI-vs-AI contact still runs 13b0 from the port's periodic balance loop rather than DOS's 3180 step scan (left for golden stability). |
| 546 | CLOSED | European trade line in the top bar is broken (probably missing strings). | FIXED 2026-09-21: the sale line ("100 furs sold for 300. 0% Tax: 0. Net: 300") takes its joining words from LABELS.TXT @CMESSAGE, but `europe_set_labels` / `europe_set_messages` ran at LABELS.TXT load time, before `europe_load`, whose `memset` wiped both handles. The typed fallbacks hid this until ee6a4b5 removed them. Both are now bound right after `europe_load` in `game_loop.c`. |
| 547 | CLOSED | Indians offered to teach a servant to be a "master colonist". This is not a valid specialty. It now names every id from its NAMES.TXT @JOB row (empty on a miss). The @LEARNMASTER helper `ai_contact_learner_skill_name` had the same gap and got the same fix. |
| 548 | CLOSED | Captured colonies have broken current projects. | FIXED 2026-09-21: the captured colony carries the AI's "no project" byte 0xFF, and DOS keeps it on capture too (`FUN_5fef_1b0e` never writes +0x94; the AI tick `FUN_5952_035e` resets it to 0xFF every turn). The port drew that state wrong: a typed "-" title and a BUY button that only said "No project". DOS `FUN_2f2b_1e46` leaves the title empty, `FUN_2f2b_21da` draws BUY only when +0x94 >= 0 (CHANGE always), and `FUN_2f2b_5fc6` hit-tests BUY under the same gate. The port now does the same, so the player picks a project with CHANGE or a pane click. |
| 549 | CLOSED | 11000 gold from burning a non-capital Arawak village seems over the top, even with Cortes. | FIXED 2026-09-21: `units_conquest_treasure_gold` banded the `FUN_5fef_31ea` peel on the game difficulty, but DOS switches on `*(char *)(*(int *)0x8d4e + 2)` — the razed tribe's Indian record `tech` (Arawak = 1 Agrarian), bound by `FUN_281f_0a42` just above (raw ~101407). An Arawak village now pays 300-800, ×1.5 with Cortes (max 1200); only Aztec/Inca (tech 2/3) reach thousands. The @LOOT/@LOOT2 %STRING1/%STRING2 were typed "native"/"village"; now the tribe name and NAMES.TXT @LEVELS column 1 by tech (row 4 "Capital" for a capital), as DOS reads DS:0x9634. |
| 550 | CLOSED | Destroyed villages seem to leave behind roads. I think they didn't do that in DOS. | FIXED 2026-09-21: DOS-checked. `FUN_4d56_00e0` starts with `FUN_281f_068c(x, y, 2, 0)` (raw 81307 → `FUN_137f_015e`), which clears only the settlement bit 0x02 on the tile; a real road (0x08) stays. The port draws a road on any settlement tile, and `col1_destroy_tribe_at` never cleared the bit, so the razed site kept its road until the next save/load. It now clears it. The campaign4 saves show no road bit under the razed villages. |
| 551 | CLOSED | Destroying an Indian settlement with a mission of your country that hasn't been burned before the village is burned needs to return the missionary used to establish the mission, respawned on the former village site. Right after the destroy, `FUN_5fef_1b0e` (raw 100667-100672) spawns a Missionary (`@UNIT` 3) for the conqueror on the village tile when the mission byte names the conqueror, with profession 0x18 (Jesuit) if the mission had the Jesuit bit. A rival nation's mission is lost. The port never did this (the Jesuit mission in campaign4 at (46,28) vanished). Ported as `units_spawn_mission_return`. The convert-join now runs after the destroy, in DOS order. |
| 552 | REFUTED | Destroying a native village also should despawn any braves associated with that village that are on the map. | REFUTED 2026-09-21: the port already does what DOS does. `FUN_4d56_00e0` (raw 81310-81319) deletes every Indian unit whose home village `+0x314a` is the razed one, wherever it stands, and shifts the higher home indexes down; `col1_destroy_tribe_at` has done the same since 2026-09-07. The campaign4 saves agree: after the razes in COLONY08→09 and 09→01, no brave is still bound to a razed village. Each village has only about one bound brave, so braves still nearby belong to the tribe's other villages, and DOS keeps those too. A regression test now covers it. |
| 553 | CLOSED | Sailing a ship into a captured colony is impossible. It should behave like any other colony you own. | FIXED 2026-09-21: capture was not the cause. A native Brave had walked into Jamestown and stood there fortified (campaign4 COLONY09/01), and the ship probe bounced the owner's ship off that foreign land unit (BLOCKED_DOMAIN). DOS `FUN_465b_0000` never lands a Brave on a foreign tile: when the destination's stack-head nation, or failing that its settlement owner (`FUN_281f_07e0` / `06be`, raw 75467-75479), is foreign, it exhausts the unit (< 3 MP, `LAB_465b_01ce`) or attacks through `FUN_5fef_1b0e` (raw 75692). `ai_native_brave_step` used to commit the 021a pick as a plain step. It now stops the Brave in place and exhausts it (the attack stays parked). `units_enter_probe_w` now docks a ship in its own colony even when foreign units are standing there, which fixes the existing saves. Land units entering that colony still meet the Brave until it walks off. |

Position-dependent inputs to that block are only the goal probes at the ship tile (FUN_521d_0116 on the 0a60 table, i.e. an ocean FOUND/MIL_EXPAND goal sitting exactly on (48,53)/(51,39)) and the nearest-colony distance; a Spanish/French FOUND goal on those ocean tiles would also explain the 0x40 via `iVar16 != 0` and, with none at (51,39), the pioneer staying aboard there (mask 0x10 alone). The G-table stance (−0x6790) gates the whole block and is 4 in the port for both continents; DOS's value is unknown. Next: dump the port's 0a60 goal table for nations 1/2 at TURN2→3 and compare the FOUND ocean tiles it produces with (48,53)/(51,39); if they differ, the 0a60 beachhead producer (raw 87983/88049) is the real #530 residue. 2026-09-22e: goal-table check done — the port's 0a60 beachhead producer writes the same nation-independent FOUND ocean list for every AI nation, including (50,39) = the French ship's DOS TURN3 goto, and nothing on (48,53)/(51,39), so the 0116 ship-tile probe is 0 in both DOS and port there; DS:0x1740 is set only by FUN_5bfb_13b0 (Euro treaty tick, unmet peers → inert); no colonies exist in TURN2 so the `local_74 < 7` term is dead. Re-deriving the DOS French path from the scores gives (54,38)→(53,38)→(52,39)→(51,39): (52,39) has no land neighbour, so DOS never saw a coast before (51,39) and France is a pure RNG-phase residue after all (the port went (52,38) on a 1-point rng margin). Spain remains the real anomaly: W,W is the only score-admissible 2-step route, (49,53) is coastal ((48,54) free land, same continent 13 as the eventual landing) and DOS did not unload there; the Spanish TURN3 ship reads state 0 / goto = own tile / plan '9' (not the 0x0c+neighbour a same-call unload leaves, cf. France), i.e. the unload happened in a separate 20e6 call after the arrival tick. Also unexplained: French soldier and both Spanish passengers read orders 0 ashore while the Dutch pair reads 1 (LAB_3558 via 465b_0c1e keeps 1). Leads: the (−2,−2) passenger-parking sentinel (FUN_1427_10be) vs the save's ship-tile coordinates may change which 0a60 / LAB_3558 gates see the passengers; and whether 20e6's unload block is entered only on a specific act-state/MP-spent combination. 2026-09-22f: LAB_3558 entry (raw 89380-89384) is unconditional for a hull outside its own colony (`cargo cap != 0 → \|\| nearest-own-colony dist != 0`), and inside it the passenger count `local_a8 = 0d38(ship,2) − 1` is taken BEFORE `FUN_281f_0948` (stack_set_xy) re-homes the chain onto the ship tile and AFTER `FUN_281f_0920` (FUN_1427_10be) — so whether the count sees parked passengers depends on 10be's parking behaviour on a hull that has already moved this turn; that ordering is the one static lead left for the Spanish (49,53) non-unload and is where a DOSBox-X watch on unit+0x3144 of a passenger during an AI hull's multi-step act would settle it in minutes. 2026-09-22g: guess A ("passenger count precedes the 0948 re-home, so the first 20e6 call after a step sees no passengers") ported and rejected: DOS runs two 20e6 calls per tile anyway (the 0x0c arrival call, then the state-0 call after the 0972 tick), so the second call still unloads at (49,53); in the port it changed nothing for France/Spain and moved the Dutch hull off (48,13) because the start-tile passengers were already homed. Reverted. Every remaining mechanism that would leave Spain aboard at (49,53) but landed at (48,53) contradicts a byte-exact reading somewhere; this row now needs the DOSBox-X watch (passenger unit+0x3144/+0x314c during the Spanish TURN2→3 hull act). Still OPEN; the fitted default stays. 2026-09-27 decomposition (S6 done): this row is five near-independent problems and should be worked, and eventually split, as such. S1 = the Spanish TURN2->3 unload gate (W,W is the only score-admissible route, (49,53) is coastal and DOS did not unload there but did at (48,53)); every static mechanism tried contradicts a byte-exact read somewhere, so this one genuinely needs the DOSBox-X watch on a passenger's +0x3144/+0x314c during that hull act, and it blocks nothing else below. S2 = the French 3-draw residue, already proven a pure RNG-stream phase (any offset k in {3,7,11,13,17,21} flips the step-2 pick; the scorer was exonerated by hand arithmetic in 22d), so it belongs in its own row against the 6d8e UI pump / music-picker `randomize`, not against ai_euro. S3 = the ashore `orders` byte (French soldier and both Spanish passengers read 0, the Dutch pair reads 1), a sharply posed question about which unload path 465b_0c1e is reached by. S4 = the missing tile-reveal owner-nibble writer on water, fully independent, statically inert for the scorer, and checkable against the TURN2/3 layer3 grids. S5 = the fit-layer deletion, which is seven call sites with seven different real replacements, not one delete: ai_euro_goals.c ship_found, ai_euro_act.c:482/706/917-918 (three separate stages: course, arrival, dispatcher wake), and the three non-opening consumers ai_euro.c:1028 and ai_euro_ship.c:2677/3182, which are the cheapest first cuts. S6 (this pass) measured what the fixtures actually pin, and the answer narrows the row considerably. (a) golden_ai_turns has no hand-written expectations at all -- every field is read from the real DOS TURN(t+1).SAV -- so there is no fitted-artefact assertion to reclassify and the original framing of S6 was wrong; the fit is load-bearing for genuine DOS facts, by memorising them. (b) `ship_found` was two arms, and only one matters: gating them separately shows the second-wave arm (1<=colonies<6, prio-4 FOUND from a live ai_euro_pick_founding_tile, no hardcoded coordinates) is dead in every fixture, while the colonies==0 fitted arm alone carries TURN3->4, TURN4->5 and TURN5->6. The dead arm is now deleted (it had no FUN_/raw cite, and the row's own survey says the only DOS primary-FOUND writers are raw 87983/88049, both ocean); golden_ai_turns 6/6, ctest 95/95, docs/ai_euro_logic_map.yaml ship_found node updated. (c) what the surviving arm supplies is exactly three facts, one per AI nation: the first-colony site. TURN6->7 passes without it, so the fit is load-bearing only for TURN3..5. With the arm off, the units still land and walk correctly -- the Dutch scout even stands on (49,14) -- but the colonist carries goto (53,14), the fit's landfall tile, instead of stopping on the site. So the fit's whole contribution is supplying the FOUND goal tile, which makes S5's opening work a goal-producer problem, separable from the ship movement/unload problems S1-S3. (d) AI_SHIP_DOS=1 does not supply those three sites either (it fails TURN2->3 as well as TURN3->4's Dutch colony), so the earlier note that the Dutch opening matches DOS exactly was about hull movement only, never about founding. The per-nation default flip suggested by that note is therefore not available yet. (e) the opening fit is two stacked layers, not one: ai_euro_recover_landfall_from_ship (a 3-entry seed-100 ship->landfall table) feeding ai_euro_06ae_first_colony_from_landfall, whose landfall->site step is itself fitted (the latitude-band offset triple (-4,0)/(-8,-4)/(-6,-5); its own header says the full multi-ring 06ae is still OPEN). Composed, they are a 3-entry ship->site lookup. Both layers' *failure* return is load-bearing at 12+ call sites (the T1.3/T1.4 notes in ai_euro.c), which is the real obstacle to replacing either: a ring search that almost always succeeds changes which branch unrelated call sites take. So S5 splits again into S5a, map each of those call sites' success/failure expectation (pure reading, no trace), and S5b, replace the two layers once the failure contract is known. 2026-09-27b, S4 done (the water owner-nibble writer). Cause found: DOS reveals once per *step*, from inside the mover. `FUN_281f_07a0` (the far thunk to the `FUN_13f1_02f8` reveal) sits in `FUN_465b_0000` unconditionally, on both of that body's move-commit arms, right after the arrival bookkeeping (0948 stack_set_xy, 084e, 07fe). The port revealed only at the turn top (`turn_reveal_fog_for_nation`, FUN_3844_00f2) plus the human order paths, so every intermediate tile of an AI multi-step move went unstamped -- which is exactly why DOS's TURN2/3 layer3 grids show 0x11/0x21/0x31 blocks along each AI hull's whole route and the port's did not. So the row's "the port has no such writer" was slightly off: `units_reveal_tile_effects` already stamps unclaimed tiles, water included and with the right settlement/rumour guards; it was simply never reached per step. Fix is one call, `units_reveal_sight_w(w, unit)` at the `units_try_move_w` commit point beside `units_vis_mask_after_move` (src/core/units_move.c). Measured against the real saves (count water tiles whose layer3 high nibble != 0xf, port vs DOS, per golden step) -- before: dos_only 52/12/19/20/17/19 for TURN1->2..TURN6->7; after: 19/4/1/3/2/7, with both_diff = 0 at every step both before and after, i.e. the port never stamps a *wrong* nation, it only under-stamped. ctest 95/95, golden_ai_turns 6/6, make golden clean. Note the regenerated smoke outputs test-saves-ai/LATE01.SAV, LATE01_POST.SAV and MID02.SAV now differ from their old committed bytes; verified this is the fix's own effect (reverting the one call regenerates them byte-identical to HEAD), not corruption. S4 residue leads, both small and independent: `FUN_13f1_02f8` has two more DOS callers the port does not reach -- `FUN_1427_06b4`, which is unit CREATE (a newly spawned unit reveals at birth), and `FUN_1427_08ea`, the placement/select path that sets the current-unit global DS:0x5392. The creation one is the likelier residue source but is not a one-liner: `units_spawn` takes only (pool, type_index, x, y) and has no map or colonies, so wiring a spawn-time reveal means threading the world through every spawn call site. The small port_only counts (17 at TURN1->2, 2 at TURN3->4 and TURN4->5, 6 at TURN6->7) are a second residue: the port now over-stamps a few tiles, so at least one of the port's reveal sites fires where DOS's does not -- most likely the turn-top walk revealing from a position DOS had already moved past. 2026-09-27c, S5 first pass: the seed-100 landfall table went from 7 live consumers to 3, and the method was to gate each consumer alone and measure rather than reason about it. Four of the seven were simply dead -- gated individually and then together, each left golden_ai_turns 6/6, the whole golden suite clean and ctest 95/95 -- and are deleted: (1) the pre-found arm of `ai_euro_try_post_found_coast_cruise` (ai_euro.c), which recovered a tip from the table and then demanded a pioneer already standing on the 06ae site; that function now simply requires a founded colony, which is the only reading its own premise supports since its cruise legs are measured off one; (2) the 06ae retry fallback in the ship found-approach arm (ai_euro_ship.c), leaving the live landfall -- the ashore soldier's or pioneer's goto, else the ship tile -- as the sole input, with `have_found` staying 0 as the intended "no found target here" signal; (3) and (4) the two fallbacks in ai_euro_act.c's first-colony course and arrival stages. The load-bearing third consumer, `ai_euro_recover_nation_landfall`, turned out not to need a DOS producer at all. Logging every one of its firings across the six golden steps showed what it was compensating for: the incoming value was ALREADY the found site, never a landfall -- Dutch (49,14), French (50,37), Spanish (45,52)/(46,54)/(46,55) -- so 06ae, whose gate wants an eastern-rim landfall (x >= 53), failed by construction, and the table then mapped the ship's tile back to the very site the caller already held. That is a type confusion (a site passed where a landfall was expected), not the "planning yanked the goto" repair the function's comment claimed. So two of its three callers (the dispatcher wake rules in ai_euro.c) are now `ai_euro_found_site_from_goto`: 06ae when the goto is a landfall, else the goto itself, which is map- and seed-independent and strictly more general than the table. Still on the table, all three load-bearing and all three the same first-colony fact: ai_euro_goals.c's opening arm, ai_euro_act.c's dispatcher wake (the `u->x`/`u->goto` pair probe), and ai_euro_ship.c's `ai_euro_recover_nation_landfall` caller. That last one resisted the goto generalization because it threads `lf_x`/`lf_y` onward into `ai_euro_resolve_first_found_tile`, which wants a landfall rather than a site -- call that S5c, and it is the next cheap step. Also worth noting for whoever picks this up: of the six fit sites measured so far across S6 and S5, four were dead code, so gate-and-measure before reasoning is the cheapest move on this row by a wide margin. 2026-09-27d, S5c: the premise was wrong and the real blocker is now named. `ai_euro_resolve_first_found_tile`'s `lf_x`/`lf_y` are not the obstacle at all -- logging its every call across the six golden steps shows it returns via its FIRST branch (`ai_goals_best_found_tile_near`, the primary FOUND goal) every single time, for all three AI nations, so the landfall parameter threaded into it is never read. The table's last consumer in ai_euro_ship.c survives for a different reason: three literal `lf_x == 53 && lf_y == 56` comparisons further down the same function. (53,56) is the seed-100 Spanish landfall, so those are Spanish-only special cases dressed up as coordinate tests, and the `ai_euro_recover_nation_landfall` call exists precisely to normalise any Spanish found site back into that literal so they fire. Rewriting the call as `ai_euro_found_site_from_goto` therefore fails TURN4->5 and TURN5->6, and that attempt was reverted. Gating the three literals separately: the first (`pioneer_at_found && !settler_aboard`, commented "SP: pioneer already on NA -- keep soldier on SE staging (TURN4->5)") is dead -- golden_ai_turns 6/6, golden suite clean, ctest 95/95, the very TURN4->5 step its comment cited included -- and is deleted. The other two are load-bearing, one golden step each. So the precise blocker for retiring the seed-100 table is now two disjuncts, not a subsystem: `!settler_aboard && !pioneer_aboard && lf_x == 53 && lf_y == 56` in the soldier arm, and `lf_x == 53 && lf_y == 56 && !settler_aboard` in the post-beachhead arm. Deliberately NOT done: swapping those literals for an explicit `nation_id == Spain` test. It would let the table die and would make the fit visible instead of hidden, but it is the same fit relabelled, and it is strictly worse off-fixture -- today a non-seed-100 map misses the table so the arms never fire, whereas a nation test would fire them everywhere. Finding a cite for what those two arms actually encode (or showing they are dead under a corrected ship/land act order) is the sub-problem; call it S5d. Running tally of fit sites measured across S6, S5 and S5c: nine measured, six dead. Next: S5d (cite or refute the two surviving (53,56) disjuncts), then S5a (map the remaining call sites' success/failure expectations). |

| 1003 | CLOSED | User-reported: Arawak braves ambushed a lone Artillery twice in campaign4 although every tribe's `alarm_by_player` toward the player was 0, and the attacking braves could not have come from the one angry village (a horseshoe landmass puts it an impassable walk away). Two causes compose. (a) The 152e threat scorer is geometric, not topological: `FUN_4cc6_03f8` gates colonies at `map_dos_dist < 7`, so the un-missioned Arawak village 41 at (33,31) reads Panama (pop 13, 16 buildings), Buenos Aires (pop 15, 16) and Potosi (pop 14, 10) as neighbours at distance 5 straight across the horseshoe's mouth, scores ~3/turn onto its own `tribe[].alarm[e]` word and ratchets to 83 with no mission on it to pay it down. That part is DOS-literal. (b) `ai_contact_raid_gate_target` then built `max_alarm = max(alarm_by_player[e], worst village friction anywhere in the nation)` — with a clause excusing mission villages below friction 80 — and handed that single scalar to every Brave of the nation (`ai_contact_raid_stage_combat` loops `brave->x/y` with no link back to the village that supplied it). So village 41's 83 licensed ambushes by the braves parked next to Trinidad, Jamestown and Buenos Aires, all three homed in the player's own Jesuit mission villages whose own friction was 1-2. DOS couples the two layers nowhere: `FUN_4d56_4528` gates on `FUN_281f_030c(bound tribe slot, euro)` (the `alarm_by_player` read) against 0x4b and never touches DS:0x54f6, and across all three decompiled exports the only DOS *gate* reads of that word are `FUN_5bfb_022e` raw 87333 (`0x7f < word`, demand-vs-gift arm) and `FUN_4962_0018` raw 94967 (`word < 0x80`, AI garrison-quota threat seed), both keyed on `unit +0x314a` = the acting Brave's own home village. The nation-wide maximum was a port construction: its own citation is `original_sources_annotated/ai/indian_raid_outcomes.md` §1, whose heading says it maps "a **reasonable** OpenCol port", with the mission clause cited to "fandom Alarm". | FIXED 2026-09-30: `ai_contact_raid_gate_target` (src/core/ai_contact_raid.c) reads `ind->alarm_by_player[e]` and nothing else; the village-friction maximum and the mission-excuse clause are gone. A second, already-dead copy of the same maximum in the `units.c` raid-repelled entry point was deleted (it built the value and discarded it — `ai_contact_pick_raid_kind` takes no alarm argument). The band values (40 gate / 55 ambush / 70 approach / 80 hot) are deliberately UNCHANGED: they belong to this pass, which is an acknowledged thin stand-in for the PARKED `FUN_4d56_2820` decision matrix, so replacing invented thresholds with different invented thresholds buys nothing — only the cross-village coupling is fixed. Test: `unit_ai_contact_core` sp_20's raid-gate case, rewritten from asserting the old coupling ("burn-band mission tribe friction should still allow raid") to pinning the DOS rule in both directions — a village word of 55 and of 85 must not raid while the nation scalar is cold, and a hot nation scalar must still raid with a cold village word. Verified the gate stays reachable: replaying `original_saves/early-game-seed-100/TURN1.SAV` headless, `indian[3].alarm[2]` climbs to 33 within 7 turns off one pop-1 AI colony, so natives are neither passive nor hair-trigger. Residual, NOT fixed: `ai_contact_pair_friction` (src/core/ai_contact_demand.c) is the same nation-wide-maximum construction gating gift refusal and the demand band; DOS's counterpart there is 022e's per-Brave home-village word, so the correct replacement needs the acting Brave in hand at those call sites. ctest 95/95, make golden clean. |

| 999 | CLOSED | User-reported: new Europe dock immigrants were inserted at the wrong end of the queue. DOS has no dock array — the dock is the unit chain on the nation's dock lane tile (x = y = nation - 0x14), `FUN_281f_07e0` answers the NEWEST unit on it (`FUN_1427_0002` walks +0x315c to the chain end), and the dock painter `FUN_38fd_15aa` fills slot 0, 1, 2 ... walking `FUN_281f_02e4` (+0x315e) back towards the oldest. So a colonist DOS *creates* in Europe (`FUN_38fd_0718` -> `FUN_281f_095c` -> `FUN_1427_06b4` -> `FUN_1427_02ca`, i.e. crosses immigrant, paid/free Recruit, Brewster pick, Train, Purchase, King mercenaries) is linked in as the newest and lands on dock slot 0. A colonist that *arrives* from the New World does the opposite: `FUN_48d3_03d0` (raw 77761-77782) ticks the shared crossing counter +0x315a of every unit on the lane (hull and passengers get it stamped together at raw 77611-77626), and for each one that reaches 0 it calls `FUN_281f_0880` (place on the port lane = link as newest) followed by `FUN_281f_08c6` = `FUN_1427_03a0`, which re-links it at the OLDEST end (+0x315e = -1) — so an arrival sorts behind everyone already waiting. The port had both backwards: every creation appended at the tail and `europe_disembark_passengers_to_dock` pushed arrivals at the front. | FIXED 2026-09-30: new `europe_dock_insert_front` (src/core/europe.c, declared in europe_internal.h) is the one slot allocator for the five creation paths in europe_dock.c (recruit, free recruit/Brewster, crosses immigrant, train, purchase); `europe_disembark_passengers_to_dock` now walks the holds forward and appends through `europe_dock_push_load`, which keeps boarding order (03d0 walks the lane newest-first and each push-to-back reverses that again). Readers of "the immigrant that just arrived" follow: turn_colony.c's crosses notify + mirror unit and units.c's Brewster mirror read `dock[0]`. Not changed: col1 import still fills the dock in save-unit-index order rather than from the Europe lane chain, so a DOS-written save's dock order is only as good as its unit array order. ctest 95/95, make golden clean. |

Testing exactly 0x1c || 0x19 as `thunk_FUN_1000_a618` (overlays.c:77796) does would therefore send the port's Free Colonists to @LEARNMASTER — a regression, not a fidelity gain. The divergence is a consequence of the port's pool alias, not an independent defect, and can only be retired together with the 0x13 alias itself. Nothing to fix; nothing left to record beyond this note. |

| 954 | CLOSED | No AI hull ever sailed back to Europe, so a Euro AI nation's Europe dock queue never drained: with port_bugs.md #953 fixed the hulls left the park immediately and never returned, and on campaign4 COLONY03/COLONY06 the waiting-unit count still climbed to 9-11 over 60 turns while the nation's colonies stood still. | 2026-09-26 Two DOS pieces were missing. It is cargo-independent and tested on every act, which is what collects an AI nation's emigrants; the port had only the 1-in-32 `LAB_521d_457e` cadence plus the Man-O-War REF arm and kept no 0x945a/0x9456 state at all. (2) The crossing: in DOS `FUN_48d3_015e` only stamps the goal (`+0x314b = 0x45`, tile into +0x314d/e, bumps DS:0x9456, raw 77712-77727) and the map->Europe teleport happens later in the SHARED goto-step mover `FUN_479b_076e` (raw 77095-77107) -> `FUN_291f_0208` -> `FUN_48d3_007a`, for any hull that ends its goto on a High Seas tile; the port's `ai_euro_ship_enter_europe` was reachable from three specific arms only, so a hull that reached the rim by any other path never crossed. Fix: `ai_euro_europe_dock_land_units` / `ai_euro_europe_lane_ships` (the two census tallies; the 5d04 hire tail's own 0x945a callback now forwards to the first), `ai_euro_20e6_3fa6_sail_home` (the 015e course setter, which stamps `col1_ai_plan = 0x45` — the port already carries DOS's +0x314b byte there — and which the 457e cadence now shares), `ai_euro_20e6_europe_dock_demand` wired into `ai_euro_act_ship_war_trade` at the DOS position (ahead of the delivery picker / 4393 haul / 457e cadence, with no war gate of its own), and the crossing gate moved to the head of `ai_euro_act_ship`, on the 0x45 stamp alone — DOS's broader "any AI nation" clause would re-cross a hull the Europe EXIT had just placed on a rim tile. `ai_euro_ship_enter_europe` clears the stamp on arrival so the park clause counts the hull instead. Measured with `tools/ai_replay` on campaign4 COLONY03 (+40 turns): the French Europe queue goes 6 -> 0 and their colony 4 -> 7 pop. Residual, and now the binding constraint for a nation with NO colonies (campaign4 English): its hull does fetch the queue, but the Dragoons it lands have nowhere to go and cannot found — that is port_bugs.md #530 / #712 (the first-colony path recognises only Pioneer/Soldier waves), not this row. |
| 955 | REFUTED | Idle AI warships burn a whole 24-thirds allotment on eight one-step wander acts that form a closed loop and end where they started (campaign4 COLONY03: English Privateer (25,43)->(24,44)->(25,45)->(26,45)->(25,44)->(24,44)->(25,43)->(26,42); French Privateer an exact period-4 cycle), every turn for ~200 turns. | 2026-09-26 REFUTED as a wander defect — the loop is DOS-faithful idle wander, so no damper was added. Evidence: (a) DOS re-enters `LAB_521d_4d2e` for the same idle hull until its MP is gone — the `FUN_521d_6d8e` inner re-dispatch (raw 93224-93312) only force-exhausts a unit after 20 consecutive acts (DS:0x2d14 > 0x14), and the 4d2e tail (`LAB_521d_589e`, raw 90375-90397) hands out a one-tile `+0x314c = 0x0c` goto without spending MP, so eight acts per turn is DOS, not a port artifact; (b) the port's scorer is a complete transcription of the DOS ship term list (base rand(1,5) + terrain<<2, high-seas -0x10, facing -2*delta^2, hostile-unit -10, fort/artillery x holds, west lean, far-probe +8, neighbour explore +2 / presence -2, LAB_52aa odds x4) with no invented term; (c) DOS has no anti-revisit or distance-from-start bias to break such a cycle either — only the soft facing penalty; (d) `AI_4D2E_TRACE` on the live saves shows only 3-5 of 8 directions are ever scorable because both hulls sit against a coastline, and a terrain/region probe confirms genuine ocean (region 1, not a lake), so the confinement is the map, not a bad open-sea test. The hulls loiter because nothing gave them a job, which was port_bugs.md #953 / #954; with those fixed the same hulls ferry the Europe queue instead. One genuine cited omission WAS found in the scorer while checking and is fixed: the `LAB_521d_5183` attack tail, raw 89033-89039, holds the unit (`local_76 = 8`) when the winning pick is an attack and `max_allotment - spent < 3`, i.e. less than one full tile of movement remains. The port skipped it on the claim that "OpenCol moves is whole moves", which is wrong — `moves` is in THIRDS — so the port let a unit attack on a 1/3 or 2/3 remainder DOS would never spend. Now gated in `ai_euro_20e6_wander_step`. It has no dedicated test: the gate only fires when the winning candidate is an attack, and this file's synthetic fixtures cannot produce one (the attack arm rejects anything under odds 0x0c outright, raw 88886-88954; Soldier-vs-Soldier-in-colony and Artillery-vs-lone-colonist were both tried over 200 RNG seeds and score `saw_foe` but never win the direction) — a note in `tests/unit/test_ai_euro_20e6.c` records that. | 967 | REFUTED | Euro AI end-leaf audit (raw 88607 / raw 85879): DOS `LAB_521d_5888` decrements the colony LABOR counter `+0x8e` **unconditionally** on a signed char (`*(char *)(*(int *)0x8542 + 0x8e) += -1`), so the counter may go negative; the port floors it at 0 (`if (labor_shortage > 0) labor_shortage--;`) at src/core/ai_euro_land.c:3680, src/core/ai_euro_land.c:3363 and src/core/colony_workers.c:505 — an un-cited clamp. The field is `uint8_t labor_shortage` (colony.h:110, save byte +0x8e per col1_save_layout.h:410), so simply dropping the clamp would wrap 0 to 0xff and read as a *huge* shortage at the unsigned compare sites, which is not DOS either: DOS's own readers compare signed (`colony.labor_shortage < 1`, cited in the comment at ai_euro_land.c:3320), where -1 behaves like 0. A faithful fix therefore needs signed (`(int8_t)`) reads at every consumer, not just an unclamped decrement. Observable difference is narrow because `FUN_5952_035e` rewrites `+0x8e = want` unconditionally each colony tick (ai_euro_goals.c:1241/1277), so a negative only survives inside one turn; within that turn the sites that distinguish -1 from 0 are the `!= 0` test at ai_euro_colony_jobs.c:2151 and repeated decrements by 2+ surplus units. |  REFUTED 2026-09-28: the clamp is dead code at both AI sites and DOS's own guard at the third. ai_euro_land.c:3363 and :3680 are only reachable with `labor_shortage >= 1` — the entry gate at :3345 requires it and its `admitted` disjunct returns at the LAB_5899 stay first, and every arm between that gate and the :3680 tail is gated on `!force_wander`, so nothing can decrement it to 0 in between. colony_workers.c:505 mirrors DOS literally: raw 87699-87701 runs the decrement inside `while (... '\0' < *(char *)(colony + 0x8e))`, with +0x1e carrying its own `!= 0` test. Dropping the clamp would also be wrong in the port's types: `labor_shortage` is uint8_t, so an unclamped decrement wraps 0 to 0xff and reads as a huge shortage at every unsigned consumer, where DOS's readers compare signed (`labor_shortage < 1`) and -1 behaves like 0. All three sites now carry the invariant as a comment. ctest 95/95, golden 98/98. |
| 968 | REFUTED | Euro AI end-leaf audit (raw 88607): at `LAB_521d_5888` DOS decrements `+0x8e` on the colony record held in the global `DS:0x8542`, i.e. whatever colony the scan last loaded; the twin site at raw 85879 is immediately preceded by `FUN_281f_09e6()` (home-colony load) so there the target is the unit's HOME colony. The port resolves the target as the colony on the unit's own tile (`colonies_id_at(ctx->colonies, u->x, u->y)`, src/core/ai_euro_land.c:3678) and no-ops when the unit stands outside a colony. If no load dominates raw 88607, the port is decrementing a different colony than DOS (or none). Needs the `DS:0x8542` writers in `FUN_521d_20e6` traced against every entry path into `LAB_521d_5888`. |  REFUTED 2026-09-28: traced the DS:0x8542 writers in FUN_521d_20e6. On the only path into LAB_521d_5888 the pointer holds `local_62` = the nearest own-nation colony to the acting unit's own tile (bound at raw 88429 from the unit's own +0x3144/45 and nation, re-asserted by FUN_281f_09e6 at raw 88588; no write between 88588 and 88607). The port's `colonies_id_at(u->x, u->y)` is equivalent there because `force_wander` is set only inside the `local_2e == 0` own-colony block (distance 0 => nearest own colony IS the colony on the tile) and all arms between that block and the tail are gated on `!force_wander`, so the unit cannot have moved. The raw 85879 'twin' is not in FUN_521d_20e6 at all — it is in FUN_4d56_4528, a sibling scorer, so it is evidence of a shared idiom only. Rationale recorded at src/core/ai_euro_land.c:3677. |

| 1004 | CLOSED | The `FUN_4cc6_00f2` escalation tail cannot fire from the four biggest alarm raisers. The port splits DOS's single 0x5b1c writer into `ai_diplo_indian_alarm_delta` (France/Pocahontas halving, 0..100 clamp, attack/war-bit clears, 5-point village-word tier clamp) and the wrapper `ai_contact_alarm_delta_00f2` (the tail: alarm == 100 while at PEACE -> `rng(0,10) <= (human ? difficulty : 1) + 1` -> burn every mission that euro holds in the nation + @INDIANBURN). DOS has no half writer: `FUN_281f_0d6c` is a bare thunk to the whole of `FUN_4cc6_00f2` (raw 80826-80917) and 0x5b1c is written nowhere else in any decompiled export. Four POSITIVE-delta sites call the bare half because they hold no `ColonizeTurnContext`: `colony_workers.c:464` (the land-work bill `difficulty+5`, the dominant early-game raiser, port_bugs.md #1002), `units_pioneer.c:194` (clear/plow/road charge), `units_combat.c:2353` (465b trespass bump) and `units_move.c:302`. Net: in the port, encroachment can pin a pair at alarm 100 forever and never burn a mission; in DOS it rolls every time the charge lands on 100. The negative-delta bare callers (`ai_diplo_indian_capital_surrender`, `effect_pocahontas_reset_alarm`) are fine — the tail needs a positive delta landing on 100. Fix shape: give the tail a context-free form (col1 + rng + messages/popup queue are all reachable from `ColonizeWorld`) and call it from the writer, or thread `w` to those four sites. Self-acknowledged in the code (`colony_workers.c` ~389, `ai_contact_demand.c` ~2375) but never filed. | FIXED 2026-10-01: the tail got a context-free entry point. `ai_contact.c` now holds `ai_contact_00f2_expel` (the alarm-100 + PEACE gate, the rng(0,10) <= cap+1 roll and the FUN_4cc6_0000 mission clear) plus two wrappers over it: `ai_contact_alarm_delta_00f2(ctx, ...)` as before, and the new `ai_contact_alarm_delta_00f2_w(w, ...)` taking a `ColonizeWorld`. `ColonizeWorld` gained `ai_popups` + `messages` (copied by `world_from_turn_ctx`; NULL on a `world_make` view = mechanics without the dialog), which is what the world form needs for @INDIANBURN; "human" there is the DS:0x543f control byte, since there is no `ctx->human_nation` override to honour and no status line to write. Three bare callers converted: `colony_workers.c` land-work bill (has `w`), `units_move.c` 465b trespass slam (has `w`; it passes a copy with `col1 = g_units_ff_col1`, the save the rest of that block already works on) and `units_pioneer.c` clear/plow/road (`units_pioneer_native_land_tail` now takes `w` + `ai_popups` + `messages`, which were already in scope at both of its call sites). The fourth site named in the row, `units_combat.c:2353`, is `units_indian_attack_alarm_vent` — its delta is NEGATIVE (the `local_a6` vent), so it never lands on 100 and correctly stays on the bare writer, as do `ai_diplo_indian_capital_surrender` and `effect_pocahontas_reset_alarm`. One behaviour guard added: the roll is skipped when there is no RNG stream, because `dos_rng_range(NULL)` answers its low bound and would have made the burn unconditional for headless callers (the two existing mission-burn test cases ran with `ctx.rng == NULL` and now seed one). `ai_contact_link_stubs.c` gained the matching slim-target stub. ctest 95/95, make golden clean. |
